Complex vs etale Abel Jacobi map for higher Chow groups and algebraicity of the zero locus of etale normal functions
Johann Bouali

Abstract
We prove, using p p -adic Hodge theory for open algebraic varieties, that for a smooth projective variety over a field k ⊂ ℂ k\subset\mathbb{C} of finite type over ℚ \mathbb{Q} , the complex abel jacobi map vanishes if the etale abel jacobi map vanishes. This implies that for a smooth projective morphism f : X → S f:X\to S of smooth complex algebraic varieties over a field k ⊂ ℂ k\subset\mathbb{C} of finite type over ℚ \mathbb{Q} and Z ∈ 𝒵 d ( X , n ) f , ∂ = 0 Z\in\mathcal{Z}^{d}(X,n)^{f,\partial=0} an algebraic cycle flat over S S whose cohomology class vanishes on fibers, the zero locus of the etale normal function associated to Z Z is contained in the zero locus of the complex normal function associated to Z Z . From the work of Saito or Charles, we deduce that the zero locus of the complex normal function associated to Z Z is defined over k ¯ \bar{k} if the zero locus of the etale normal function associated to Z Z is not empty. We also prove an algebraicity result for the zero locus of an etale normal function associated to an algebraic cycle over a field k k of finite type over ℚ \mathbb{Q} . By the way, we get for a smooth morphism f : X → S f:X\to S of smooth complex algebraic varieties over a subfield k ⊂ ℂ k\subset\mathbb{C} of finite type over ℚ \mathbb{Q} , the locus of Hodge Tate classes inside the locus of Hodge classes of f f .



1
Introduction

Let
X
be a connected smooth projective variety over
{\mathbb{C}}
.
The Abel-Jacobi map associates to a cycle
Z\in\mathcal{Z}^{d}(X)
homologically equivalent to 0 of codimension
d
,
a point
AJ(X)(Z)\in J^{d}(X)
in the intermediate Jacobian of
X
.
In family, if
f:X\to S
is a smooth projective morphism of smooth connected complex varieties and

Z\in\mathcal{Z}^{d}(X)
is a relative cycle on
S
of codimension
d
homologicaly trivial on fibers,
the Abel-Jacobi map provides a holomorphic and horizontal section of the relative intermediate Jacobian :

\nu_{Z}:S\to J^{d}(X/S)
called the associated normal function.
By a Brosnan-Pearlstein theorem, the zero-locus
V(\nu_{Z})\subset S
and more generally the torsion locus

V(\nu_{Z})\subset V_{tors}(\nu_{Z})\subset S
of
\nu_{Z}
is an algebraic subvariety (
[
3
]
).
If
X/S
and
Z
are defined over a subfield
k\subset\mathbb{C}
,
we conjecture in the spirit of the Bloch-Beilinson conjectures,
that the zero-locus of the normal function is also defined over
k
(see
[
7
]
).

On the other hand, if
k
is of finite type over
{\mathbb{Q}}
,
X
is a connected smooth projective variety over
k
,
and
p
is a prime number, we can define via the continuous étale cohomology with
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
coefficients
an etale Abel-Jacobi map which associate to a cycle
Z\in\mathcal{Z}^{d}(X,n)
homologically equivalent to 0 of codimension
d
,
an element
AJ^{et,p}(X)(Z)\in H^{1}(G,H^{2d-1-n}(X_{\bar{k}},\mathbb{Z}_{p}))
of the first degree Galois cohomology of
the absolute galois group
G:=Gal(\bar{k}/k)
with value in the etale cohomology of
X_{\bar{k}}
with
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
coefficients.
In family, we get, for
f:X\to S
a smooth projective morphism of smooth connected varieties over
k

and
Z\in\mathcal{Z}^{d}(X,n)
a relative cycle on
S
of codimension
d
homologicaly trivial on fibers,
a normal function
\nu_{Z}^{et,p}
associated to
Z\in\mathcal{Z}^{d}(X,n)

and thus a zero-locus
V(\nu_{Z}^{et,p})\subset S
and more generally
a torsion locus
V(\nu_{Z}^{et,p})\subset V_{tors}(\nu_{Z}^{et,p})\subset S
of
\nu_{Z}^{et}

which are subsets of closed points of
S
.
For
\sigma:k\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
an embedding, and
V\subset S
a subset, we denote

V_{\mathbb{C}}:=\pi_{k/\mathbb{C}}(S)^{-1}(V)\subset S_{\mathbb{C}}

where
\pi_{k/\mathbb{C}}(S):S_{\mathbb{C}}\to S
is the projection.

Let
k
of finite type over
{\mathbb{Q}}
and

f:X\to S
a smooth projective morphism of smooth connected varieties over
k
. Let
Z\in\mathcal{Z}^{d}(X)
a relative cycle.
F. Charles then proves that for any embedding
\sigma:k\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}

•

assuming that
R^{2d-1}f^{an}_{*}(\mathbb{C})
has no global sections
then
V(\nu_{Z}^{et,p})_{\mathbb{C}}=V(\nu_{Z})
(
[
7
]
)

•

if
V(\nu_{Z})(\bar{k})
is non-empty then it is defined over
k
(
[
8
]
).

In this work, we show that, for a field
k
of finite type over
\mathbb{Q}
,

X
a connected smooth projective variety over
k
,

Z\in\mathcal{Z}^{d}(X,*)
an (higher) algebraic cycle homologically equivalent to 0 of codimension
d
,
and
p\in\mathbb{N}
a prime number,
if
AJ^{et,p}(X)(Z)=0
then
AJ_{\sigma}(X)(Z):=AJ(X_{\mathbb{C}})(Z_{\mathbb{C}})=0

for any embedding
\sigma:k\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
(c.f. theorem
6
).
This implies by definition that for
f:X\to S
a smooth projective morphism of smooth connected varieties over
a field
k
of finite type over
\mathbb{Q}
,
and
Z\in\mathcal{Z}^{d}(X,*)
a relative cycle on
S
of codimension
d
homologicaly trivial on fibers,

V_{tors}(\nu_{Z}^{et,p})_{\mathbb{C}}\subset V_{tors}(\nu_{Z})

for any embedding
\sigma:k\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
without any assumption (c.f. corollary
2
(i)).
We deduce that if
V(\nu_{Z}^{et,p})
is not empty,

V(\nu_{Z})
is defined over the algebraic closure
\bar{k}
of
k
(c.f. corollary
2
(ii)).

The proof of theorem
6
uses
p
-adic Hodge theory for open varieties
to relate de Rham cohomology and its Hodge filtration
to
p
-adic étale coholomogy with its Galois action.
Theorem
6
follows indeed from the fact that
by proposition
5
(i), proposition
4
and proposition
1
,
we have for a field
k
of finite type over
\mathbb{Q}
,
U\in\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(k)
,
and embeddings
\sigma:k\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
,
\sigma_{p}:k\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}_{p}
for a prime
p\in\mathbb{N}
,
for each
j,l\in\mathbb{Z}
, a canonical injective map

H^{j}\iota_{p,ev}^{G,l}(U):H_{et}^{j}(U_{\bar{k}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})(l)^{G}\hookrightarrow F^{l}H^{j}(U^{an}_{\mathbb{C}},2i\pi\mathbb{Q})\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{Q}_{p},\;\alpha\mapsto H^{j}\iota_{p,ev}^{G,l}(U)(\alpha):=ev(U)(w(\alpha)),

which is by construction functorial in
U\in\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(k)
(see theorem
1
).
More precisely, if the étale Abel-Jacobi image of a cycle
Z
is zero,

•

there exist a Galois invariant class
\alpha
in the étale cohomology of

((X\times\square^{n})\backslash Z)_{\bar{k}}
with non-zero boundary,
where
\bar{k}
is an algebraic closure of
k
,

•

then, by
p
adic Hodge theory for
(X\times\square^{n})\backslash Z
,

\alpha
define a logarithmic de Rham class
w(\alpha)_{L}
laying inside the right degree of the Hodge filtration of

((X\times\square^{n})\backslash Z)_{\hat{k}_{\sigma_{p}}}
where
\hat{k}_{\sigma_{p}}\subset\mathbb{C}_{p}

is the
p
-adic completion of
k
with respect to an embedding
\sigma_{p}:k\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}_{p}

(c.f.proposition
5
(i) and proposition
4
)

•

taking the image of this class by the complex period map with respect to an embedding

\sigma^{\prime}:k^{\prime}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
extending the given embedding
\sigma:k\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}

where
k^{\prime}\subset\hat{k}_{\sigma_{p}}
is a subfield over which
w(\alpha)_{L}
is defined,
we get a Betti cohomology class
ev((X\times\square^{n})\backslash Z)(w(\alpha)_{L})

of
((X\times\square^{n})\backslash Z)^{an}_{\mathbb{C}}
with
2i\pi\mathbb{Q}
coefficients
(c.f. proposition
1
),

•

this last class
(1/2i\pi)ev((X\times\square^{n})\backslash Z)(w(\alpha)_{L})

induce a splitting of the localization exact sequence of mixed Hodge structures :

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow H^{2d-1-n}(X^{an}_{\mathbb{C}},\mathbb{Q})\xrightarrow{j^{*}}H^{2d-1}(((X\times\square^{n})\backslash Z)^{an}_{\mathbb{C}},\mathbb{Q})^{[Z]}\xrightarrow{\partial}H^{2d}_{Z}((X\times\square^{n})^{an}_{\mathbb{C}},\mathbb{Q})^{[Z]}=\mathbb{Q}^{Hdg}(d)\rightarrow 0,

which means that the complex Abel-Jacobi image de
Z
is zero.

By the way, since for
X\in\mathop{\rm PSmVar}\nolimits(k)
,
\iota_{p,ev}^{G,d}(X)
is compatible with cycle class maps,
we get in particular that Hodge conjecture implies Tate conjecture.
In particular, we get Tate conjecture for divisors for smooth projective varieties
over fields of characteristic zero.

In section 6, we show that, for

f:X\to S
a smooth projective morphism of connected smooth varieties over a field
k
of finite type over
\mathbb{Q}
,

Z\in\mathcal{Z}^{d}(X,*)
an (higher) algebraic cycle homologically equivalent to 0 of codimension
d
, and
p
a prime number
for all expect finitely many prime numbers
p\in\mathbb{N}

V_{tors}(\nu_{Z}^{et,p})=T\cap S_{(0)}\subset S,

where
T\subset S
is the image of closed points of a constructible algebraic subset of

E_{DR}^{2d-1}(((X\times\square^{*})\backslash|Z|)_{\hat{k}_{\sigma_{p}}}/S_{\hat{k}_{\sigma_{p}}}):=H^{2d-1}\int_{f}(O_{((X\times\square^{*})\backslash|Z|)_{\hat{k}_{\sigma_{p}}}},F_{b})\in\mathop{\rm Vect}\nolimits_{fil}(S_{\hat{k}_{\sigma_{p}}})

by the projection

p_{S}:E_{DR}^{2d-1}(((X\times\square^{n})\backslash|Z|)_{\hat{k}_{\sigma_{p}}}/S_{\hat{k}_{\sigma_{p}}})\to S_{\hat{k}_{\sigma_{p}}}
,
where
\hat{k}_{\sigma_{p}}\subset\mathbb{C}_{p}

is the completion of
k
with respect to
\sigma_{p}
(c.f. definition
8
and theorem
7
(i)).
We also give a local version for
V_{tors}(\nu_{Z,\sigma_{p}}^{et,p})\subset S
(c.f. theorem
7
(ii)).
The proof use on the one hand proposition
5
(ii), and on the other hand
De Yong alterations to get a stratification

S=\sqcup_{\alpha\in\Lambda}S^{\alpha}
by locally closed subsets
and alterations
\pi^{\alpha}:(X_{S^{\alpha}}\times\square^{n})^{\alpha}\to X_{S^{\alpha}}\times\square^{n}
such that

f\circ\pi^{\alpha}:((X\times\square^{n})^{\alpha}_{\hat{k}_{\sigma_{p}}},\pi^{\alpha,-1}(|Z|))\to S_{\hat{k}_{\sigma_{p}}}^{\alpha}

is a semi-stable morphism. We then use the
p
adic semi-stable comparison theorem
for semi-stable morphisms
f^{\prime}:(X^{\prime},D^{\prime})\to S^{\prime}
, with
S^{\prime},X^{\prime},D^{\prime}\in\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(\hat{k}_{\sigma_{p}})
, that is satisfying

•

f^{\prime}:X^{\prime}\to S
is smooth projective,
D^{\prime}\subset X^{\prime}
is a normal crossing divisor,

•

for all
s\in S^{\prime}
,
D^{\prime}_{s}\subset X^{\prime}_{s}
is a normal crossing divisor and

(X^{\prime}_{s},D^{\prime}_{s})
has integral model with semi-stable reduction,

(or more generally for log smooth morphism of schemes over
(O_{\hat{k}_{\sigma_{p}}},N_{\mathcal{O}})
),
which gives, for each
j\in\mathbb{Z}
a canonical filtered isomorphism

H^{j}f^{\prime}_{*}\alpha(U^{\prime}):R^{j}f^{\prime}_{*}\mathbb{Z}_{p,U_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}^{{}^{\prime}et}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}O\mathbb{B}_{st,S^{\prime}}\xrightarrow{\sim}R^{j}f^{\prime}_{*Hdg}(O_{U^{\prime}},F_{b})\otimes_{O_{S^{\prime}}}O\mathbb{B}_{st,S^{\prime}}.

which is for each
s\in S^{\prime}
compatible with the action of the Galois group
Gal(\mathbb{C}_{p}/k(s))
,
the Frobenius and the monodromy:
for all but finitely many primes
p
,
\hat{k}_{\sigma_{p}}(s)
is unramified
for all embeddings
\sigma_{p}:k\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}_{p}
and all
s\in S^{\prime}
, so that we get a Frobenius
action on
R^{j}f^{\prime}_{*Hdg}(O_{U^{\prime}},F_{b})_{s}=H^{j}_{DR}(U^{\prime}_{s})
for all
s\in S^{\prime}
.

This also give (see theorem
8
) together with proposition
5
(ii) and proposition
1
,
for
f:X\to S
a smooth morphism, with
S,X
smooth over a subfield

\sigma:k\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
of finite type over
\mathbb{Q}
, and
p
any but finitely many prime numbers
and
\sigma_{p}:k\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}_{p}
an embedding,
the locus of Hodge-Tate classes

(H^{j}Rf_{*\mathbb{Q}_{p,X_{\bar{k}}^{et}}}(d))^{G}\subset H^{j}Rf_{*\mathbb{Q}_{p,X_{\bar{k}}^{et}}}
,
where
G=Gal(\bar{k}/k)
and
\bar{k}\subset\mathbb{C}
is the algebraic closure of
k
in
\mathbb{C}
,

\displaystyle\iota_{ev}^{G,d}(X/S):\pi_{k/\mathbb{C}}(S)^{*}(H^{j}Rf_{*\mathbb{Q}_{p,X_{\bar{k}}^{et}}}(d))^{G}\xrightarrow{\sim}

\displaystyle<F^{d}E^{j}_{DR}(X/S)\cap(\sqcup_{\alpha\in\Lambda}((E^{j}_{DR}(X_{\hat{k}_{\sigma_{p}}}^{\alpha}/S_{\hat{k}_{\sigma_{p}}}^{\alpha})\otimes_{O_{S_{\hat{k}_{\sigma_{p}}}}}O\mathbb{B}_{st,S_{\hat{k}_{\sigma_{p}}}^{\alpha}})^{\phi,N}))>_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}

\displaystyle\hookrightarrow(F^{d}E_{DR}(X_{\mathbb{C}}/S_{\mathbb{C}})\cap R^{j}f_{*}\mathbb{Q}_{X^{an}_{\mathbb{C}}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}=:HL_{j,d}(X_{\mathbb{C}}/S_{\mathbb{C}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{Q}_{p},

\displaystyle\alpha_{s}\mapsto ev(X_{k(s)})((1/2i\pi)w(\alpha_{s})_{k(s)}),\,s^{\prime}=\pi_{k/\mathbb{C}}^{-1}(s):k(s)\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C},\,s\in S

inside the locus of Hodge classes

HL_{j,d,\sigma}(X/S):=HL_{j,d}(X_{\mathbb{C}}/S_{\mathbb{C}})\subset E^{j}_{DR}(X_{\mathbb{C}}/S_{\mathbb{C}})
,
which is a countable union of algebraic subvarieties (
[
3
]
), where

•

f:X\xrightarrow{j}\bar{X}\xrightarrow{f}S
is a compactification of
f
where
\bar{X}\in\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(k)
,

j
is an open embedding and
\bar{f}
is proper,

•

E^{j}_{DR}(X/S):=H^{j}\int_{\bar{f}}j_{*Hdg}(O_{X},F_{b})
and

E^{j}_{DR}(X_{\mathbb{C}}/S_{\mathbb{C}}):=H^{j}\int_{\bar{f}}j_{*Hdg}(O_{X_{\mathbb{C}}},F_{b})

are the filtered algebraic holonomic
D
-modules over
S
and
S_{\mathbb{C}}
respectively,

•

S=\sqcup_{\alpha\in\Lambda}S^{\alpha}
is a stratification by locally closed algebraic subsets,

\Lambda
being a finite set,

•

\pi^{\alpha}:X^{\alpha}\to X_{S^{\alpha}}
being alterations, in particular we get sub vector bundles

\pi^{\alpha,*}:E^{j}_{DR}(X_{S^{\alpha}}/S^{\alpha}):=E^{j}_{DR}(X/S)_{|S^{\alpha}}\hookrightarrow E^{j}_{DR}(X^{\alpha}/S^{\alpha})
,

•

\pi_{k/\hat{k}_{\sigma_{p}}}(S^{\alpha})^{*}E^{j}_{DR}(X^{\alpha}/S^{\alpha})\subset E^{j}_{DR}(X^{\alpha}_{\hat{k}_{\sigma_{p}}}/S^{\alpha}_{\hat{k}_{\sigma_{p}}})

is the canonical subset of closed points,
\hat{k}_{\sigma_{p}}\subset\mathbb{C}_{p}
being the
p
adic completion of
k

with respect to
\sigma_{p}
,

•

\pi_{k/\mathbb{C}}(S):S_{\mathbb{C}}\to S
and
\pi_{k/\hat{k}_{\sigma_{p}}}(S):S_{\hat{k}_{\sigma_{p}}}\to S

are the projections.

Preliminaries and Notations

•

Denote by
\mathop{\rm Top}\nolimits
the category of topological spaces and
\mathop{\rm RTop}\nolimits
the category of ringed spaces.

•

Denote by
\mathop{\rm Cat}\nolimits
the category of small categories and
\mathop{\rm RCat}\nolimits
the category of ringed topos.

•

For
\mathcal{S}\in\mathop{\rm Cat}\nolimits
and
X\in\mathcal{S}
, we denote
\mathcal{S}/X\in\mathop{\rm Cat}\nolimits
the category whose
objects are
Y/X:=(Y,f)
with
Y\in\mathcal{S}
and
f:Y\to X
is a morphism in
\mathcal{S}
, and whose morphisms

\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits((Y^{\prime},f^{\prime}),(Y,f))
consists of
g:Y^{\prime}\to Y
in
\mathcal{S}
such that
f\circ g=f^{\prime}
.

•

For
\mathcal{S}\in\mathop{\rm Cat}\nolimits
denote
\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits\mathcal{S}:=\mathop{\rm Fun}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z},\mathcal{S})
is the category of graded objects.

•

Denote by
\mathop{\rm Ab}\nolimits
the category of abelian groups.
For
R
a ring denote by
\mathop{\rm Mod}\nolimits(R)
the category of (left)
R
modules.
We have then the forgetful functor
o_{R}:\mathop{\rm Mod}\nolimits(R)\to\mathop{\rm Ab}\nolimits
.

•

Denote by
\mathop{\rm AbCat}\nolimits
the category of small abelian categories.

•

For
(\mathcal{S},O_{S})\in\mathop{\rm RCat}\nolimits
a ringed topos, we denote by

–

\mathop{\rm PSh}\nolimits(\mathcal{S})
the category of presheaves of
O_{S}
modules on
\mathcal{S}
and

\mathop{\rm PSh}\nolimits_{O_{S}}(\mathcal{S})
the category of presheaves of
O_{S}
modules on
\mathcal{S}
,
whose objects are
\mathop{\rm PSh}\nolimits_{O_{S}}(\mathcal{S})^{0}:=\left\{(M,m),M\in\mathop{\rm PSh}\nolimits(\mathcal{S}),m:M\otimes O_{S}\to M\right\}
,
together with the forgetful functor
o:\mathop{\rm PSh}\nolimits(\mathcal{S})\to\mathop{\rm PSh}\nolimits_{O_{S}}(\mathcal{S})
,

–

C(\mathcal{S})=C(\mathop{\rm PSh}\nolimits(\mathcal{S}))
and
C_{O_{S}}(\mathcal{S})=C(\mathop{\rm PSh}\nolimits_{O_{S}}(\mathcal{S}))

the big abelian category of complexes of presheaves of
O_{S}
modules on
\mathcal{S}
,

–

C_{O_{S}(2)fil}(\mathcal{S}):=C_{(2)fil}(\mathop{\rm PSh}\nolimits_{O_{S}}(\mathcal{S}))\subset C(\mathop{\rm PSh}\nolimits_{O_{S}}(\mathcal{S}),F,W)
,
the big abelian category of (bi)filtered complexes of presheaves of
O_{S}
modules on
\mathcal{S}

such that the filtration is biregular and
\mathop{\rm PSh}\nolimits_{O_{S}(2)fil}(\mathcal{S})=(\mathop{\rm PSh}\nolimits_{O_{S}}(\mathcal{S}),F,W)
.

•

Let
(\mathcal{S},O_{S})\in\mathop{\rm RCat}\nolimits
a ringed topos with topology
\tau
. For
F\in C_{O_{S}}(\mathcal{S})
,
we denote by
k:F\to E_{\tau}(F)
the canonical flasque resolution in
C_{O_{S}}(\mathcal{S})
(see
[
5
]
).
In particular for
X\in\mathcal{S}
,
H^{*}(X,E_{\tau}(F))\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{H}_{\tau}^{*}(X,F)
.

•

For
f:\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{S}
a morphism with
\mathcal{S},\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\in\mathop{\rm RCat}\nolimits
,
endowed with topology
\tau
and
\tau^{\prime}
respectively, we denote for
F\in C_{O_{S}}(\mathcal{S})
and each
j\in\mathbb{Z}
,

–

f^{*}:=H^{j}\Gamma(\mathcal{S},k\circ\mathop{\rm ad}\nolimits(f^{*},f_{*})(F)):\mathbb{H}^{j}(\mathcal{S},F)\to\mathbb{H}^{j}(\mathcal{S}^{\prime},f^{*}F)
,

–

f^{*}:=H^{j}\Gamma(\mathcal{S},k\circ\mathop{\rm ad}\nolimits(f^{*mod},f_{*})(F)):\mathbb{H}^{j}(\mathcal{S},F)\to\mathbb{H}^{j}(\mathcal{S}^{\prime},f^{*mod}F)
,

the canonical maps.

•

For
\mathcal{X}\in\mathop{\rm Cat}\nolimits
a (pre)site and
p
a prime number, we consider the full subcategory

\mathop{\rm PSh}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathcal{X})\subset\mathop{\rm PSh}\nolimits(\mathbb{N}\times\mathcal{X}),\;\;F=(F_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}},\;p^{n}F_{n}=0,\;F_{n+1}/p^{n}\xrightarrow{\sim}F_{n}

C_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathcal{X}):=C(\mathop{\rm PSh}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathcal{X}))\subset C(\mathbb{N}\times\mathcal{X})
and

\mathbb{Z}_{p}:=\mathbb{Z}_{p,\mathcal{X}}:=((\mathbb{Z}/p^{*}\mathbb{Z})_{\mathcal{X}})\in\mathop{\rm PSh}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathcal{X})

the diagram of constant presheaves on
\mathcal{X}
.

•

Let
f:\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{X}
a morphism of (pre)site with
\mathcal{X},\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\in\mathop{\rm Cat}\nolimits
.
We will consider for
F=(F_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\in C_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathcal{X})
the canonical map in
C(\mathcal{X}^{\prime})

T(f^{*},\varprojlim)(F):f^{*}\varprojlim_{n\in\mathbb{N}}F_{n}\to\varprojlim_{n\in\mathbb{N}}f^{*}F_{n}

Recall that filtered colimits do NOT commute with infinite limits in general. In particular,
for
f:\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{X}
a morphism of (pre)site and

F=(F_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\in\mathop{\rm PSh}\nolimits_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathcal{X})
,

\varprojlim_{n\in\mathbb{N}}f^{*}F_{n}
is NOT isomorphic to
f^{*}\varprojlim_{n\in\mathbb{N}}F_{n}

in
\mathop{\rm PSh}\nolimits(\mathcal{X}^{\prime})
in general.

•

Denote by
\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits\subset\mathop{\rm RTop}\nolimits
the subcategory of schemes (the morphisms are the morphisms of locally ringed spaces).
We denote by
\mathop{\rm PSch}\nolimits\subset\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits
the full subcategory of proper schemes.
For
X\in\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits
, we denote by

–

\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits^{ft}/X\subset\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits/X
the full subcategory consisting of objects

X^{\prime}/X=(X^{\prime},f)\in\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits/X
such that
f:X^{\prime}\to X
is an morphism of finite type

–

X^{et}\subset\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits^{ft}/X
the full subcategory consisting of objects

U/X=(X,h)\in\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits/X
such that
h:U\to X
is an etale morphism.

–

X^{sm}\subset\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits^{ft}/X
the full subcategory consisting of objects

U/X=(X,h)\in\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits/X
such that
h:U\to X
is a smooth morphism.

For a field
k
, we consider
\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits/k:=\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits/\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits k
the category of schemes over
\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits k
. We then denote by

–

\mathop{\rm Var}\nolimits(k)=\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits^{ft}/k\subset\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits/k
the full subcategory consisting of algebraic varieties over
k
,
i.e. schemes of finite type over
k
,

–

\mathop{\rm PVar}\nolimits(k)\subset\mathop{\rm QPVar}\nolimits(k)\subset\mathop{\rm Var}\nolimits(k)
the full subcategories consisting of quasi-projective varieties and projective varieties respectively,

–

\mathop{\rm PSmVar}\nolimits(k)\subset\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(k)\subset\mathop{\rm Var}\nolimits(k)
the full subcategories consisting of smooth varieties and smooth projective varieties respectively.

For a morphism of field
\sigma:k\hookrightarrow K
, we have the extention of scalar functor

\displaystyle\otimes_{k}K:\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits/k\to\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits/K,\;X\mapsto X_{K}:=X_{K,\sigma}:=X\otimes_{k}K,\;(f:X^{\prime}\to X)\mapsto(f_{K}:=f\otimes I:X^{\prime}_{K}\to X_{K}).

which is left ajoint to the restriction of scalar

\displaystyle Res_{k/K}:\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits/K\to\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits/k,\;X=(X,a_{X})\mapsto X=(X,\sigma\circ a_{X}),\;(f:X^{\prime}\to X)\mapsto(f:X^{\prime}\to X)

The adjonction maps are

–

for
X\in\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits/k
, the projection
\pi_{k/K}(X):X_{K}\to X
in
\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits/k
,
for
X=\cup_{i}X_{i}
an affine open cover with
X_{i}=\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(A_{i})
we have by definition
\pi_{k/K}(X_{i})=n_{k/K}(A_{i})
,

–

for
X\in\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits/K
,
I\times\Delta_{K}:X\hookrightarrow X_{K}=X\times_{K}K\otimes_{k}K
in
\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits/K
,
where
\Delta_{K}:K\otimes_{k}K\to K
is the diagonal which is given by for
x,y\in K
,
\Delta_{K}(x,y)=x-y
.

The extention of scalar functor restrict to a functor

\displaystyle\otimes_{k}K:\mathop{\rm Var}\nolimits(k)\to\mathop{\rm Var}\nolimits(K),\;X\mapsto X_{K}:=X_{K,\sigma}:=X\otimes_{k}K,\;(f:X^{\prime}\to X)\mapsto(f_{K}:=f\otimes I:X^{\prime}_{K}\to X_{K}).

and for
X\in\mathop{\rm Var}\nolimits(k)
we have
\pi_{k/K}(X):X_{K}\to X
the projection in
\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits/k
.
An algebraic variety
X\in\mathop{\rm Var}\nolimits(K)
is said to be defined over
k
if there exists
X_{0}\in\mathop{\rm Var}\nolimits(k)

such that
X\simeq X_{0}\otimes_{k}K
in
\mathop{\rm Var}\nolimits(K)
.
For
X=(X,a_{X})\in\mathop{\rm Var}\nolimits(k)
, we have
\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits^{ft}/X=\mathop{\rm Var}\nolimits(k)/X
since for
f:X^{\prime}\to X
a morphism of schemes of finite type,

(X^{\prime},a_{X}\circ f)\in\mathop{\rm Var}\nolimits(k)
is the unique structure of variety over
k
of
X^{\prime}\in\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits
such that
f
becomes a morphism
of algebraic varieties over
k
, in particular we have

–

X^{et}\subset\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits^{ft}/X=\mathop{\rm Var}\nolimits(k)/X
,

–

X^{sm}\subset\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits^{ft}/X=\mathop{\rm Var}\nolimits(k)/X
.

A morphism
f:X^{\prime}\to X
with
X,X^{\prime}\in\mathop{\rm Var}\nolimits(K)
is said to be defined over
k
if

X\simeq X_{0}\otimes_{k}K
and
X^{\prime}\simeq X^{\prime}_{0}\otimes_{k}K
are defined over
k
and

\Gamma_{f}=\Gamma_{f_{0}}\otimes_{k}K\subset X^{\prime}\times X
is defined over
k
, so that
f_{0}\otimes_{k}K=f
with
f_{0}:X^{\prime}_{0}\to X_{0}
.

•

For
X\in\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits
and
s\in\mathbb{N}
, we denote by
X_{(s)}\subset X
its points of dimension
s
,
in particular
X_{(0)}\subset X
are the closed points of
X
.

•

For
X\in\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits
and
k
a field we denote by
X(k):=\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits_{Sch}(\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits k,X)
the
k
points of
X
.
We get
X(k)_{in}\subset X
the image of the
k
-points of
X
.
For
k\subset k^{\prime}
a subfield,
\mathbb{A}^{N}_{k^{\prime}}(k)_{in}=k^{N}\subset k^{{}^{\prime}N}\subset\mathbb{A}^{N}_{k^{\prime}}
and

\mathbb{A}^{N}_{k}(k^{\prime})_{in}=\pi_{k/k^{\prime}}(\mathbb{A}^{N}_{k})(k^{{}^{\prime}N})\subset\mathbb{A}^{N}_{k}
.

•

For
X\in\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits
, we denote
X^{pet}\subset\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits/X
the pro etale site (see
[
2
]
)
which is the full subcategory of
\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits/X
whose object consists of weakly etale maps
U\to X
(that is flat maps

U\to X
such that
\Delta_{U}:U\to U\times_{X}U
is also flat) and whose topology is generated by fpqc covers.
We then have the canonical morphism of site

\nu_{X}:X^{pet}\to X,(U\to X)\mapsto(U\to X)

For
F\in C(X^{et})
,

\mathop{\rm ad}\nolimits(\nu_{X}^{*},R\nu_{X*})(F):F\to R\nu_{X*}\nu_{X}^{*}F

is an isomorphism in
D(X^{et})
, in particular, for each
n\in\mathbb{Z}

\nu_{X}^{*}:=H^{n}\Gamma(X,k):\mathbb{H}^{n}_{et}(X,F)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{H}^{n}_{pet}(X,\nu_{X}^{*}F)

are isomorphisms, where

k:=k\circ\mathop{\rm ad}\nolimits(\nu_{X}^{*},\nu_{X*})(E_{et}(F)):E_{et}(F)\to\nu_{X*}E_{pet}(\nu_{X}^{*}F)

is the canonical map in
C(X^{et})
which is a quasi-isomorphism.
For
X\in\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits
, we denote

–

\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}_{X}:=\varprojlim_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\nu_{X}^{*}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z})_{X^{et}}\in\mathop{\rm PSh}\nolimits(X^{pet})

the constant presheaf on
\mathcal{X}
,

–

l_{p,\mathcal{X}}:=(p(*)):\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}_{X}\to\nu_{X}^{*}(\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z})_{X^{et}}

the projection map in
\mathop{\rm PSh}\nolimits(\mathbb{N}\times\mathcal{X}^{pet})
.

•

Let
k
a field of characteristic zero and
k_{0}\subset k
a subfield.
We say that
k
is of finite type over
k_{0}
if
k
is generated as a field by
k_{0}
and a finite set

\left\{\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{r}\right\}\subset k
of elements of
k
, that is
k=k_{0}(\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{r})
.
If
k
is of finite type over
k_{0}
then it is of finite transcendence degree
d\in\mathbb{N}
over
k_{0}

and
k=k_{0}(\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{d})(\alpha_{d+1})
with
\left\{\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{d+1}\right\}\subset k

such that
k_{0}(\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{d})=k_{0}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d})
and

\alpha_{d+1}
is an algebraic element of
k
over
k_{0}(\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{d})
.
Note that if
k
is of finite type over
k_{0}
then it is NOT algebraicly closed.
We denote
\bar{k}
the algebraic closure of
k
. Then
\bar{k}
is also transcendence degree
d
over
k
.

•

Let
C
a field of characteristic zero. Let
X\in\mathop{\rm Var}\nolimits(C)
. Then there exist a subfield
k\subset C

of finite type over
\mathbb{Q}
such that
X
is defined over
k
that is
X\simeq X_{0}\otimes_{k}\mathbb{C}

with
X_{0}\in\mathop{\rm Var}\nolimits(k)
.

•

Let
k\subset\bar{\mathbb{Q}}
be a number field, i.e. a finite extension of
\mathbb{Q}
.
There exists a finite set of prime number
\delta(k)
such that for all prime number
p\in\mathbb{N}\backslash\delta(k)

we have for all each embedding
\sigma_{p}:k\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}_{p}

\mathbb{Q}_{p}\cap k=\mathbb{Q}
.

Let
X\in\mathop{\rm Var}\nolimits(k)
. Considering its De Rham complex
\Omega_{X}^{\bullet}:=DR(X)(O_{X}):=\Omega_{X/k}^{\bullet}
,
we have for
j\in\mathbb{Z}
its De Rham cohomology
H^{j}_{DR}(X):=\mathbb{H}^{j}(X,\Omega^{\bullet}_{X})
.
If
X\in\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(k)
, then
H^{j}_{DR}(X)=\mathbb{H}_{et}^{j}(X,\Omega^{\bullet}_{X})

since
\Omega^{\bullet}\in C(\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(k))
is
\mathbb{A}^{1}
local and admits transfert (see
[
5
]
).

•

Let
X\in\mathop{\rm Var}\nolimits(k)
. Let
X=\cup_{i=1}^{s}X_{i}
an open affine cover.
For
I\subset[1,\ldots,s]
, we denote
X_{I}:=\cap_{i\in I}X_{i}
.
We get
X_{\bullet}\in\mathop{\rm Fun}\nolimits(P([1,\ldots,s]),\mathop{\rm Var}\nolimits(k))
.
Since quasi-coherent sheaves on affine noetherian schemes are acyclic, we have
for each
j\in\mathbb{Z}
,
H^{j}_{DR}(X)=\Gamma(X_{\bullet},\Omega^{\bullet}_{X^{\bullet}})
.

x\in K
such that
|x|\leq 1
.

For
X\in\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits
and
p
a prime number,
we denote by
c:\hat{X}^{(p)}\to X
the morphism in
\mathop{\rm RTop}\nolimits
which is the completion along the ideal generated by
p
.

•

Let
K
a field which is complete with respect to a
p
-adic norm and
X\in\mathop{\rm PVar}\nolimits(K)
projective.
For
X^{\mathcal{O}}\in\mathop{\rm PSch}\nolimits/O_{K}
an integral model of
X
, i.e. satisfying
X^{\mathcal{O}}\otimes_{O_{K}}K=X
,
we consider the morphism in
\mathop{\rm RTop}\nolimits

c:=(c\otimes I):\hat{X}^{(p)}:=\hat{X}^{\mathcal{O},(p)}\otimes_{O_{K}}K\to X^{\mathcal{O}}\otimes_{O_{K}}K=X.

We have then, by GAGA (c.f. EGA 3), for
F\in\mathop{\rm Coh}\nolimits_{O_{X}}(X)
a coherent sheaf of
O_{X}
module,

c^{*}:H^{k}(X^{\mathcal{O}},F)\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{k}(\hat{X}^{\mathcal{O},(p)},c^{*mod}F)
for all
k\in\mathbb{Z}
,
in particular

c^{*}:\mathbb{H}^{k}(X,\Omega_{X}^{\bullet\geq l})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{H}^{k}(\hat{X}^{(p)},\Omega_{\hat{X}^{(p)}}^{\bullet\geq l})

for all
k,l\in\mathbb{Z}
.

•

Denote by
\mathop{\rm AnSp}\nolimits(\mathbb{C})\subset\mathop{\rm RTop}\nolimits
the full subcategory of analytic spaces over
\mathbb{C}
,
and by
\mathop{\rm AnSm}\nolimits(\mathbb{C})\subset\mathop{\rm AnSp}\nolimits(\mathbb{C})
the full subcategory of smooth analytic spaces (i.e. complex analytic manifold).
For
X\in\mathop{\rm AnSp}\nolimits(\mathbb{C})
, we denote by
X^{et}\subset\mathop{\rm AnSp}\nolimits(\mathbb{C})/X

the full subcategory consisting of objects
U/X=(X,h)\in\mathop{\rm AnSp}\nolimits(\mathbb{C})/X
such that
h:U\to X
is an etale morphism.
By the Weirstrass preparation theorem (or the implicit function theorem if
U
and
X
are smooth),
a morphism
r:U\to X
with
U,X\in\mathop{\rm AnSp}\nolimits(\mathbb{C})
is etale if and only if it is an isomorphism local.
Hence for
X\in\mathop{\rm AnSp}\nolimits(\mathbb{C})
, the morphism of site
\pi_{X}:X^{et}\to X
is an isomorphism of site.

Denote by
\mathop{\rm CW}\nolimits\subset\mathop{\rm Top}\nolimits
the full subcategory of
CW
complexes.
Denote by
\mathop{\rm Diff}\nolimits(\mathbb{R})\subset\mathop{\rm RTop}\nolimits
the full subcategory of differentiable (real) manifold.

Let
k\subset\mathbb{C}
a subfield. For
X\in\mathop{\rm Var}\nolimits(k)
, we denote by

\mathop{\rm an}\nolimits_{X}:X^{an}_{\mathbb{C}}\xrightarrow{\sim}X^{an,et}_{\mathbb{C}}\xrightarrow{\mathop{\rm an}\nolimits_{X^{et}_{\mathbb{C}}}}X^{et}_{\mathbb{C}}\xrightarrow{\pi_{k/\mathbb{C}}(X^{et})}X^{et},

the morphism of site given by the analytical functor.

Let
k\subset\mathbb{C}
a subfield. For
X\in\mathop{\rm Var}\nolimits(k)
, we denote by

\alpha(X):\mathbb{C}_{X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an}}\hookrightarrow\Omega^{\bullet}_{X^{an}_{\mathbb{C}}}

the canonical embedding in
C(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an})
. It induces the embedding in
C(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an})

\beta(X):2i\pi\mathbb{Z}_{X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an}}\xrightarrow{\iota_{2i\pi\mathbb{Z}/\mathbb{C},X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an}}}\mathbb{C}_{X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an}}\xrightarrow{\alpha(X)}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X^{an}_{\mathbb{C}}}

For
X\in\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(k)
,
\alpha(X)
is a quasi-isomorphism by the holomorphic Poincare lemma.

For
X\in\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits
noetherian irreducible and
d\in\mathbb{N}

\mathcal{Z}^{d}(X)
the group of algebraic cycles of codimension
d
,
which is the free abelian group generated by irreducible closed subsets of codimension
d
.

noetherian irreducible and
d\in\mathbb{N}

\mathcal{Z}^{d}(X,\bullet)\subset\mathcal{Z}^{d}(X\times\square^{\bullet})
the Bloch cycle complex
which is the subcomplex which consists of algebraic cycles which intersect
\square^{*}
properly.

For
X\in\mathop{\rm Var}\nolimits(k)
irreducible and
d,n\in\mathbb{N}
, we denote by

\mathcal{Z}^{d}(X,n)^{\partial=0}_{hom}\subset\mathcal{Z}^{d}(X,\bullet)^{\partial=0}
the
the subabelian group consisting of algebraic cycles which are homologicaly trivial.

For
f:X\to S
a dominant morphism with
S,X\in\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits
noetherian irreducible and
d\in\mathbb{N}
, we denote by

\mathcal{Z}^{d}(X,\bullet)^{f}\subset\mathcal{Z}^{d}(X,\bullet)

the subcomplex consisting of algebraic cycles which are flat over
S
.

–

For
f:X\to S
a dominant morphism with

irreducible and

we denote by

\mathcal{Z}^{d}(X,n)_{fhom}^{f,\partial=0}\subset\mathcal{Z}^{d}(X,n)^{f,\partial=0}

the subabelian group consisting of algebraic cycles which are flat over
S
and homological trivial on fibers.

For
X\in\mathop{\rm Var}\nolimits(k)
and
Z\subset X
a closed subset, denoting
j:X\backslash Z\hookrightarrow X
the open complementary,
we will consider

\Gamma^{\vee}_{Z}\mathbb{Z}_{X}:=\mathop{\rm Cone}\nolimits(\mathop{\rm ad}\nolimits(j_{\sharp},j^{*})(\mathbb{Z}_{X}):\mathbb{Z}_{X}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{X})\in C(\mathop{\rm Var}\nolimits(k)^{sm}/X)

and denote for short
\gamma^{\vee}_{Z}:=\gamma^{\vee}_{Z}(\mathbb{Z}_{X}):\mathbb{Z}_{X}\to\Gamma^{\vee}_{Z}\mathbb{Z}_{X}

the canonical map in
C(\mathop{\rm Var}\nolimits(k)^{sm}/X)
. Denote
a_{X}:X\to\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits k
the structural map.
For
X\in\mathop{\rm Var}\nolimits(k)
and
Z\subset X
a closed subset, we have the motive of
X
with support in
Z
defined as

M_{Z}(X):=a_{X!}\Gamma^{\vee}_{Z}a_{X}^{!}\mathbb{Z}\in\mathop{\rm DA}\nolimits(k).

If
X\in\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(k)
, we will also consider

a_{X\sharp}\Gamma^{\vee}_{Z}\mathbb{Z}_{X}:=\mathop{\rm Cone}\nolimits(a_{X\sharp}\circ\mathop{\rm ad}\nolimits(j_{\sharp},j^{*})(\mathbb{Z}_{X}):\mathbb{Z}(U)\hookrightarrow\mathbb{Z}(X))=:\mathbb{Z}(X,X\backslash Z)\in C(\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(k)).

Then for
X\in\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(k)
and
Z\subset X
a closed subset

M_{Z}(X):=a_{X!}\Gamma^{\vee}_{Z}a_{X}^{!}\mathbb{Z}=a_{X\sharp}\Gamma^{\vee}_{Z}\mathbb{Z}_{X}=:\mathbb{Z}(X,X\backslash Z)\in\mathop{\rm DA}\nolimits(k).

We denote
\mathbb{I}^{n}:=[0,1]^{n}\in\mathop{\rm Diff}\nolimits(\mathbb{R})
(with boundary).
For
X\in\mathop{\rm Top}\nolimits
and
R
a ring, we consider its singular cochain complex

C^{*}_{\mathop{\rm sing}\nolimits}(X,R):=(\mathbb{Z}\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits_{\mathop{\rm Top}\nolimits}(\mathbb{I}^{*},X)^{\vee})\otimes R

and for
l\in\mathbb{Z}
its singular cohomology
H^{l}_{\mathop{\rm sing}\nolimits}(X,R):=H^{n}C^{*}_{\mathop{\rm sing}\nolimits}(X,R)
.
In particular, we get by functoriality the complex

C^{*}_{X,R\mathop{\rm sing}\nolimits}\in C_{R}(X),\;(U\subset X)\mapsto C^{*}_{\mathop{\rm sing}\nolimits}(U,R)

We will consider the canonical embedding

C^{*}\iota_{2i\pi\mathbb{Z}/\mathbb{C}}(X):C^{*}_{\mathop{\rm sing}\nolimits}(X,2i\pi\mathbb{Z})\hookrightarrow C^{*}_{\mathop{\rm sing}\nolimits}(X,\mathbb{C}),\,\alpha\mapsto\alpha\otimes 1

whose image consists of cochains
\alpha\in C^{j}_{\mathop{\rm sing}\nolimits}(X,\mathbb{C})
such that
\alpha(\gamma)\in 2i\pi\mathbb{Z}

for all
\gamma\in\mathbb{Z}\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits_{\mathop{\rm Top}\nolimits}(\mathbb{I}^{*},X)
.
We get by functoriality the embedding in
C(X)

\displaystyle C^{*}\iota_{2i\pi\mathbb{Z}/\mathbb{C},X}:C^{*}_{X,2i\pi\mathbb{Z},\mathop{\rm sing}\nolimits}\hookrightarrow C^{*}_{X,\mathbb{C},\mathop{\rm sing}\nolimits},

\displaystyle(U\subset X)\mapsto(C^{*}\iota_{2i\pi\mathbb{Z}/\mathbb{C}}(U):C^{*}_{\mathop{\rm sing}\nolimits}(U,2i\pi\mathbb{Z})\hookrightarrow C^{*}_{\mathop{\rm sing}\nolimits}(U,\mathbb{C}))

We recall we have

–

For
X\in\mathop{\rm Top}\nolimits
locally contractile, e.g.
X\in\mathop{\rm CW}\nolimits
, and
R
a ring, the inclusion in
C_{R}(X)

c_{X}:R_{X}\to C^{*}_{X,R\mathop{\rm sing}\nolimits}
is by definition an equivalence top local and that we get
by the small chain theorem, for all
l\in\mathbb{Z}
, an isomorphism

H^{l}c_{X}:H^{l}(X,R_{X})\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{l}_{\mathop{\rm sing}\nolimits}(X,R)
.

–

For
X\in\mathop{\rm Diff}\nolimits(\mathbb{R})
, the restriction map

r_{X}:\mathbb{Z}\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits_{\mathop{\rm Diff}\nolimits(\mathbb{R})}(\mathbb{I}^{*},X)^{\vee}\to C^{*}_{\mathop{\rm sing}\nolimits}(X,R),\;w\mapsto w:(\phi\mapsto w(\phi))

is a quasi-isomorphism by Whitney approximation theorem.

Let
X\in\mathop{\rm AnSm}\nolimits(\mathbb{C})
. Let
X=\cup_{i=1}^{r}\mathbb{D}_{i}
an open cover with
\mathbb{D}_{i}\simeq D(0,1)^{d}
.
Since a convex open subset of
\mathbb{C}^{d}
is biholomorphic to an open ball we have

\mathbb{D}_{I}:=\cap_{i\in I}\mathbb{D}_{i}\simeq D(0,1)^{d}
(where
d
is the dimension of a connected component of
X
).
We get
\mathbb{D}_{\bullet}\in\mathop{\rm Fun}\nolimits(P([1,\ldots,r]),\mathop{\rm AnSm}\nolimits(\mathbb{C}))
.

•

For
k
a field, we denote by
\mathop{\rm Vect}\nolimits(k)
the category of vector spaces and

\mathop{\rm Vect}\nolimits_{fil}(k)
the category of filtered vector spaces.
Let
k\subset K
a field extension of field of characteristic zero.

–

For
(V,F)\in\mathop{\rm Vect}\nolimits_{fil}(k)
, we get a filtered
K
vector space

(V\otimes_{k}K,F)\in\mathop{\rm Vect}\nolimits_{fil}(K)
by
F^{j}(V\otimes_{k}K):=(F^{j}V)\otimes_{k}K
.
In this case, we say that the filtration
F
on
V\otimes_{k}K
is defined over
k
.

–

For
(V^{\prime},F)\in\mathop{\rm Vect}\nolimits_{fil}(K)
and
h:V\otimes_{k}K\xrightarrow{\sim}V^{\prime}
and isomorphism of
K
vector space, we get

(V,F_{h})\in\mathop{\rm Vect}\nolimits_{fil}(k)
by
F_{h}^{j}V:=h^{-1}(F^{j}V^{\prime})\cap V

(considering the canonical embedding
n:V\hookrightarrow V\otimes_{k}K
,
n(v):=v\otimes 1
).

–

For
(V,F)\in\mathop{\rm Vect}\nolimits_{fil}(k)
, we have
F^{j}(V\otimes_{k}K)\cap V=F^{j}V
.

–

For
(V^{\prime},F)\in\mathop{\rm Vect}\nolimits_{fil}(K)
and
h:V\otimes_{k}K\xrightarrow{\sim}V^{\prime}
and isomorphism of
K
vector space,
we have
h((F_{h}^{j}V)\otimes_{k}K)\subset F^{j}V^{\prime}
. Of course this inclusion is NOT an equality in general.
The filtration
F
on
V^{\prime}
is NOT defined over
k
in general.

•

We also consider

–

\mathop{\rm Top}\nolimits_{2}
the category whose objects are couples
(X,Y)
with
X\in\mathop{\rm Top}\nolimits
and
Y\subset X
a subset
and whose set of morphisms
\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits((X^{\prime},Y^{\prime}),(X,Y))
consists of
f:X^{\prime}\to X
continuous such that
Y^{\prime}\subset f^{-1}(Y)

(i.e.
f(Y^{\prime})\subset Y
),

–

\mathop{\rm RTop}\nolimits_{2}
the category whose objects are couples
(X,Y)
with
X=(X,O_{X})\in\mathop{\rm RTop}\nolimits
and
Y\subset X
a subset
and whose set of morphisms
\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits((X^{\prime},Y^{\prime}),(X,Y))
consists of
f:X^{\prime}\to X
of ringed spaces such that
Y^{\prime}\subset f^{-1}(Y)
,

–

\mathop{\rm Top}\nolimits^{2}
the category whose objects are couples
(X,Z)
with
X\in\mathop{\rm Top}\nolimits
and
Z\subset X
a closed subset
and whose set of morphisms
\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits((X^{\prime},Z^{\prime}),(X,Z))
consists of
f:X^{\prime}\to X
continuous such that
f^{-1}(Z)\subset Z^{\prime}

(i.e.
f(X^{\prime}\backslash Z^{\prime})\subset X\backslash Z
),
in particular we have the canonical functor
\mathop{\rm Top}\nolimits^{2}\to\mathop{\rm Top}\nolimits_{2}
,
(X,Z)\mapsto(X,X\backslash Z)
,

–

\mathop{\rm RTop}\nolimits^{2}
the category whose objects are couples
(X,Z)
with
X=(X,O_{X})\in\mathop{\rm RTop}\nolimits
and
Z\subset X
a closed subset
and whose set of morphisms
\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits((X^{\prime},Z^{\prime}),(X,Z))
consists of
f:X^{\prime}\to X
of ringed spaces such that
f^{-1}(Z)\subset Z^{\prime}
,
in particular we have the canonical functor
\mathop{\rm RTop}\nolimits^{2}\to\mathop{\rm RTop}\nolimits_{2}
,
(X,Z)\mapsto(X,X\backslash Z)
.

A (generalized) cohomology theory is in particular a functor
H^{*}:\mathop{\rm Top}\nolimits_{2}\to\mathop{\rm GrAb}\nolimits
, e.g singular cohomology

H^{*}_{\mathop{\rm sing}\nolimits}:\mathop{\rm Top}\nolimits^{2}\to\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits\mathop{\rm Ab}\nolimits,(X,Y)\mapsto H^{*}_{\mathop{\rm sing}\nolimits}(X,Y,R).

where
R
is a commutative ring.
It restrict to a functor
H^{*}:\mathop{\rm Top}\nolimits^{2}\to\mathop{\rm GrAb}\nolimits,(X,Z)\mapsto H^{*}_{Z}(X):=H^{*}(X,X\backslash Z)
.

•

Denote
\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits^{2}\subset\mathop{\rm RTop}\nolimits^{2}
the subcategory
whose objects are couples
(X,Z)
with
X=(X,O_{X})\in\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits
and
Z\subset X
a closed subset
and whose set of morphisms
\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits((X^{\prime},Z^{\prime}),(X,Z))
consists of
f:X^{\prime}\to X
of locally ringed spaces
such that
f^{-1}(Z)\subset Z^{\prime}
.

•

Let
k
a field of characteristic zero.
Denote
\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits^{2}(k)\subset\mathop{\rm Var}\nolimits^{2}(k)\subset\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits^{2}/k
the full subcategories
whose objects are
(X,Z)
with
X\in\mathop{\rm Var}\nolimits(k)
, resp.
X\in\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(k)
, and
Z\subset X
is a closed subset,
and whose morphisms
\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits((X^{\prime},Z^{\prime})\to(X,Z))
consists of
f:X^{\prime}\to X
of schemes over
k

such that
f^{-1}(Z)\subset Z^{\prime}
.

•

Let
k
a field of characteristic zero. Let

H^{*}:\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits^{2}(k)\to\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits\mathop{\rm AbCat}\nolimits,(X,Z)\mapsto H^{*}_{Z}(X)

a mixed Weil cohomology theory in sense of
[
9
]

(e.g. (filtered) De Rham, etale or Betti cohomology, Hodge or
p

For
X\in\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(k)
and
Z\subset X
a closed subset, we denote

H^{*}_{Z}(X)^{0}:=\ker(H^{*}_{Z}(X)\to H^{*}(X)).

For

,
we consider the subobject
H^{2d-1}(U)^{[Z]}\subset H^{2d-1}(U)
where

j:U:=(X\times\square^{n})\backslash|Z|\hookrightarrow X\times\square^{n}
is the complementary open subset,
given by the pullback by
H^{2d}_{Z}(X\times\square^{n})^{[Z]}:=[Z]\subset H^{2d}_{Z}(X\times\square^{n})^{0}

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{H^{2d-1}(U)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\scriptstyle{j^{*}}

\scriptstyle{\subset}

of the first row exact sequence. In particular the second row is also an exact sequence.

•

We denote by
\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits
the category of log schemes whose objects are couples
(X,M):=(X,M,\alpha)

where
X=(X,O_{X})\in\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits
,
M\in\mathop{\rm Shv}\nolimits(X)
is a sheaf of monoid and
\alpha:M\to O_{X}
is a morphism of sheaves of monoid.
In particular we have a canonical functor

\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits^{2}\to\mathop{\rm logSch}\nolimits,(X,Z)\mapsto(X,M_{Z}),M_{Z}:=(f\in O_{X}s.t.f_{|X\backslash Z}\in O_{X\backslash Z}^{*})\subset O_{X}

Let
k
a field of characteristic zero.
We denote by
\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits\mathop{\rm Var}\nolimits(k)\subset\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits/k
the full subcategory of log varieties.

•

For
k
a field, we denote by
\mathop{\rm Vect}\nolimits(k)=\mathop{\rm Mod}\nolimits(k)
the category of vector spaces over
k

and
C(k):=C(\mathop{\rm Vect}\nolimits(k))
the category of complexes of vector spaces over
k
,
by
\mathop{\rm Vect}\nolimits_{fil}(k)
the category of filtered vector spaces over
k
and
C_{fil}(k):=C_{fil}(\mathop{\rm Vect}\nolimits(k))

the category of filtered complexes of vector spaces over
k
.

•

We assume all field have a transcendence basis at most countable, so that field of characteristic zero
admit embeddings in
\mathbb{C}
and in
\mathbb{C}_{p}
for each prime number
p\in\mathbb{N}
.

We have the followings facts :

•

Let
k
a field of characteristic zero. Denote
G:=Gal(\bar{k}/k)
its absolute Galois group.
Then the functor

\Gamma(\bar{k})(-):\mathop{\rm PSh}\nolimits(k^{et})\to\mathop{\rm Mod}\nolimits(\bar{k},G),\;F\mapsto\Gamma(\bar{k},F)

is an equivalence of category whose inverse is

G(-):\mathop{\rm Mod}\nolimits(\bar{k},G)\to\mathop{\rm PSh}\nolimits(k^{et}),\;V\mapsto G(V):=V:=((k^{\prime}/k)\mapsto V^{Aut(k^{\prime}/k)}).

In particular, for each
V\in\mathop{\rm Mod}\nolimits(\bar{k},G)
and
j\in\mathbb{Z}
, we get an isomorphism

H^{j}G(V):H^{j}(G,V)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathop{\rm Ext}\nolimits_{G}^{j}(\bar{k},V).

•

Let
k\subset K
a field extension.

–

Let
X\in\mathop{\rm Var}\nolimits(k)
.
We have then the canonical isomorphism in
C_{Aut(K/k),fil}(X_{K})

w(k/K):(\Omega^{\bullet}_{X}\otimes_{k}K,F_{b})\xrightarrow{\sim}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{K}},F_{b})

given by the universal property of derivation of a ring.

–

Let
X\in\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(k)
.
Let
\bar{X}\in\mathop{\rm PSmVar}\nolimits(k)
a smooth compactification of
X
with
D:=\bar{X}\backslash X
a normal crossing divisor.
We have then the canonical isomorphism in
C_{Aut(K/k),fil}(\bar{X}_{K})

w(k/K):(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\bar{X}}(\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits D)\otimes_{k}K,F_{b})\xrightarrow{\sim}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\bar{X}_{K}}(\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits D),F_{b})

given by the preceeding point. In particular, we get for all
j,l\in\mathbb{Z}
,

*

F^{l}H^{j}w(k/K):F^{l}H^{j}_{DR}(X)\otimes_{k}K\xrightarrow{\sim}F^{l}H^{j}_{DR}(X_{K})
,

*

H^{j}w(k/K):H^{j}_{DR}(X)\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{j}_{DR}(X_{K})^{G}
.

•

An affine scheme
U\in\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits
is said to be
w
-contractible if any faithfully flat weakly etale map
V\to U
,

V\in\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits
, admits a section. We will use the facts that (see
[
2
]
):

–

Any scheme
X\in\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits
admits a pro-etale cover
(r_{i}:X_{i}\to X)_{i\in I}

with for each
i\in I
,
X_{i}
a
w
-contractile affine scheme and
r_{i}:X_{i}\to X
a weakly etale map.
For
X\in\mathop{\rm Var}\nolimits(k)
with
k
a field, we may assume
I
finite since the topological space
X
is then quasi-compact.

–

If
U\in\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits
is a
w
-contractible affine scheme, then for any sheaf
F\in\mathop{\rm Shv}\nolimits(U^{pet})
,

H_{pet}^{i}(U,F)=0
for
i\neq 0
since
\Gamma(U,-)
is an exact functor.

We introduce the logarithmic De Rham complexes

Definition 1
.

(i)

Let
X=(X,O_{X})\in\mathop{\rm RCat}\nolimits
a ringed topos, we have in
C(X)
the subcomplex of presheaves of abelian groups

\displaystyle OL_{X}:\Omega^{\bullet}_{X,\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits}\hookrightarrow\Omega_{X}^{\bullet},\;\mbox{s.t. for}\;X^{o}\in X\;\mbox{and}\;p\in\mathbb{N},\,p\geq 1,

\displaystyle\Omega^{p}_{X,\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits}(X^{0}):=<df_{\alpha_{1}}/f_{\alpha_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge df_{\alpha_{p}}/f_{\alpha_{p}},f_{\alpha_{k}}\in\Gamma(X^{o},O_{X})^{*}>\subset\Omega^{p}_{X}(X^{0}),

where
\Omega_{X}^{\bullet}:=DR(X)(O_{X})\in C(X)
is the De Rham complex and

\Gamma(X^{o},O_{X})^{*}\subset\Gamma(X^{o},O_{X})
is the multiplicative subgroup
consisting of invertible elements for the multiplication,
here
<,>
stand for the sub-abelian group generated by. By definition, for
w\in\Omega^{p}_{X}(X^{o})
,

w\in\Omega^{p}_{X,\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits}(X^{o})
if and only if there exists
(n_{i})_{1\leq i\leq s}\in\mathbb{Z}
and

(f_{i,\alpha_{k}})_{1\leq i\leq s,1\leq k\leq p}\in\Gamma(X^{o},O_{X})^{*}
such that

w=\sum_{1\leq i\leq s}n_{i}df_{i,\alpha_{1}}/f_{i,\alpha_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge df_{i,\alpha_{p}}/f_{i,\alpha_{p}}\in\Omega^{p}_{X}(X^{o}).

For
p=0
, we set
\Omega^{0}_{X,\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits}:=\mathbb{Z}
.
Let
f:X^{\prime}=(X^{\prime},O_{X^{\prime}})\to X=(X,O_{X})
a morphism with
X,X^{\prime}\in\mathop{\rm RCat}\nolimits
.
Consider the morphism
\Omega(f):\Omega_{X}^{\bullet}\to f_{*}\Omega_{X^{\prime}}^{\bullet}
in
C(X)
.
Then,
\Omega(f)(\Omega^{\bullet}_{X,\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits})\subset f_{*}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X^{\prime},\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits}
.

(ii)

For
k
a field, we get from (i), for
X\in\mathop{\rm Var}\nolimits(k)
, the embedding in
C(X)

OL_{X}:\Omega^{\bullet}_{X,\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits}\hookrightarrow\Omega_{X}^{\bullet}:=\Omega^{\bullet}_{X/k},

such that, for
X^{o}\subset X
an open subset and
w\in\Omega^{p}_{X}(X^{o})
,

w\in\Omega^{p}_{X,\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits}(X^{o})
if and only if there exists
(n_{i})_{1\leq i\leq s}\in\mathbb{Z}
and

(f_{i,\alpha_{k}})_{1\leq i\leq s,1\leq k\leq p}\in\Gamma(X^{o},O_{X})^{*}
such that

w=\sum_{1\leq i\leq s}n_{i}df_{i,\alpha_{1}}/f_{i,\alpha_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge df_{i,\alpha_{p}}/f_{i,\alpha_{p}}\in\Omega^{p}_{X}(X^{o}),

and for
p=0
,
\Omega^{0}_{X,\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits}:=\mathbb{Z}
. Let
k
a field. We get an embedding in
C(\mathop{\rm Var}\nolimits(k))

\displaystyle OL:\Omega^{\bullet}_{/k,\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits}\hookrightarrow\Omega^{\bullet}_{/k},\;\mbox{given by},\,\mbox{for}\,X\in\mathop{\rm Var}\nolimits(k),

\displaystyle OL(X):=OL_{X}:\Omega^{\bullet}_{/k,\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits}(X):=\Gamma(X,\Omega^{\bullet}_{X,\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits})\hookrightarrow\Gamma(X,\Omega^{\bullet}_{X})=:\Omega^{\bullet}_{/k}(X)

and its restriction to
\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(k)\subset\mathop{\rm Var}\nolimits(k)
.

(iii)

Let
K
a field of characteristic zero which is complete for a
p
-adic norm. Let
X\in\mathop{\rm Var}\nolimits(K)
.
Let
X^{\mathcal{O}}\in\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits/O_{K}
an integral model of
X
, in particular

X^{\mathcal{O}}\otimes_{O_{K}}K=X
. We have then the morphisms of sites

and

such that

.
We then consider the embedding of

OL_{X}:=OL_{X}\circ\iota:\Omega_{X^{et},\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits,\mathcal{O}}^{\bullet}:=r^{*}\Omega_{X^{\mathcal{O},et},\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits}^{\bullet}\hookrightarrow\Omega_{X^{et},\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits}^{\bullet}\hookrightarrow\Omega_{X^{et}}^{\bullet}

consisting of integral logarithimc De Rham forms,
with
\iota:r^{*}\Omega_{X^{\mathcal{O},et},\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits}^{\bullet}\hookrightarrow\Omega_{X^{et},\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits}^{\bullet}
.
We will also consider the embedding of

\displaystyle OL_{\hat{X}^{\mathcal{O},(p)}}:=(OL_{X^{\mathcal{O}}/p^{n}})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}:\Omega_{\hat{X}^{(p)},\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits,\mathcal{O}}^{\bullet}:=\varprojlim_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\nu_{X^{\mathcal{O}}}^{*}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X^{\mathcal{O},et}/p^{n},\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits}

\displaystyle\hookrightarrow\Omega_{\hat{X}^{\mathcal{O},(p)}}^{\bullet}:=\varprojlim_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\nu_{X^{\mathcal{O}}}^{*}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X^{\mathcal{O},et}/p^{n}/(O_{K}/p^{n})}

Note that

are NOT the pullback of etale sheaves by
\nu_{X}
.
We consider

We get the embedding of

and its restriction to
