\mathop{\rm an}\nolimits_{X}:X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an}\simeq X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an,et}\xrightarrow{\mathop{\rm an}\nolimits_{X}}X_{\mathbb{C}}^{et}\xrightarrow{\pi_{\bar{k}/\mathbb{C}}(X_{\bar{k}}^{et})}X_{\bar{k}}^{et},\;\;\mathop{\rm an}\nolimits_{X}:X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an}\simeq X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an,et}\xrightarrow{\mathop{\rm an}\nolimits_{X}}X_{\mathbb{C}}^{et}\xrightarrow{\pi_{k/\mathbb{C}}(X^{et})}X^{et}

In particular, we get for
X\in\mathop{\rm Var}\nolimits(k)
and
j\in\mathbb{Z}
, the canonical map

\displaystyle T(X):=H^{j}T(X):H^{j}_{\mathop{\rm sing}\nolimits}(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an},\mathbb{Z})\xrightarrow{H^{j}(/p^{*})}H^{j}_{\mathop{\rm sing}\nolimits}(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an},\mathbb{Z}_{p})\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{j}(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an},\mathbb{Z}_{p})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{(\mathop{\rm an}\nolimits_{X}^{*})^{-1}}H^{j}_{et}(X_{\mathbb{C}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})\xrightarrow{(\pi_{\bar{k}/\mathbb{C}}(X)^{*})^{-1}}H^{j}_{et}(X_{\bar{k}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})

for each
j\in\mathbb{Z}
, where
H^{j}(\mathop{\rm an}\nolimits_{X}^{*})
is an isomorphism by the comparaison theorem between etale cohomology
and Betti cohomology with torsion coefficients (see SGA4).

Let
p\in\mathbb{N}
a prime number. Let
k\subset\mathbb{C}_{p}
a subfield. Let
X\in\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(k)
.
Take a compactification
j:X\hookrightarrow\bar{X}
with
\bar{X}\in\mathop{\rm PSmVar}\nolimits(k)

such that
D:=\bar{X}\backslash X\subset\bar{X}
is a normal crossing divisor. Then for each
j\in\mathbb{Z}
,

H^{j}_{DR}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}})=H^{j}\Gamma(\bar{X}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}},E_{zar}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}}(\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits D_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}),F_{b}))=H^{j}\Gamma(\bar{X}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}},E_{pet}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}}(\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits D_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}),F_{b}))\in\mathop{\rm Vect}\nolimits_{fil}(\mathbb{C}_{p}).

By the complex case which follows from the Hodge decomposition, we see
after taking an isomorphism
\sigma:\mathbb{C}_{p}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{C}
that
the spectral sequence associated to

\Gamma(\bar{X}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}},E_{zar}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}}(\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits D_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}),F_{b}))\in C_{fil}(\mathbb{C}_{p})

is
E_{1}
degenerate. However there is no canonical splitting of the filtration on
H^{j}_{DR}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}})

(it depend on the choice of a basis of this
\mathbb{C}_{p}
vector space, or on the choice of such an isomorphism
\sigma
).
Then the canonical embedding in
C_{fil}(\bar{X}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}^{pet})

OL_{X}:=j_{*}OL_{X}:j_{*}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}},\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits,\mathcal{O}}\hookrightarrow\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}}(\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits D_{\mathbb{C}_{p}})\hookrightarrow j_{*}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}}

induces in cohomology

\displaystyle H^{j}OL_{X}:\mathbb{H}_{pet}^{j}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}},\Omega^{\bullet\geq l}_{X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}},\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits,\mathcal{O}})\to\mathbb{H}_{pet}^{j}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}},\Omega^{\bullet\geq l}_{X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}}(\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits D_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}))\xrightarrow{=}F^{l}H^{j}_{DR}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}})\hookrightarrow H^{j}_{DR}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}).

Similarly, the canonical embedding in
C_{fil}(\bar{X}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}^{pet})

m\circ(OL_{X}\otimes I):j_{*}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}},\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits,\mathcal{O}}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})\hookrightarrow\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}}(\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits D_{\mathbb{C}_{p}})\hookrightarrow j_{*}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}}

induces in cohomology

\displaystyle H^{j}(m\circ(OL_{X}\otimes I)):\mathbb{H}_{pet}^{j}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}},\Omega^{\bullet\geq l}_{X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}},\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits,\mathcal{O}}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to

\displaystyle\mathbb{H}_{pet}^{j}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}},\Omega^{\bullet\geq l}_{X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}}(\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits D_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}))\xrightarrow{=}F^{l}H^{j}_{DR}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}})\hookrightarrow H^{j}_{DR}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}).

Consider, for each
j\in\mathbb{Z}
, a (non canonical) splitting

\theta_{j}(X):H^{j}_{DR}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}})\xrightarrow{\sim}\oplus_{0\leq l\leq j}H_{zar}^{l}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}},\Omega^{j-l}_{X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}}(\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits D_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}))=\oplus_{0\leq l\leq j}H_{pet}^{l}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}},\Omega^{j-l}_{X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}}(\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits D_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}))

in
\mathop{\rm Vect}\nolimits(\mathbb{C}_{p})
. We then have the following map in
\mathop{\rm Vect}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}_{p})

\displaystyle OL_{X}^{\theta_{j}}:=\theta_{j}(X)^{-1}\circ(\oplus_{0\leq l\leq j}H^{l}OL_{X}^{l-j}):

\displaystyle\mathbb{H}_{pet}^{j}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}},\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}},\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits,\mathcal{O}}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})=\oplus_{0\leq l\leq j}H_{pet}^{l}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}},\Omega^{j-l}_{X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}},\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits,\mathcal{O}}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\oplus_{0\leq l\leq j}H^{l}OL_{X}^{l-j}}\oplus_{0\leq l\leq j}H_{pet}^{l}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}},\Omega^{j-l}_{X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}}(\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits D_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}))\xrightarrow{\theta_{j}(X)^{-1}}H^{j}_{DR}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}),

where the equality follows from the fact that the differential of

\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}},\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits,\mathcal{O}}
vanishes (all the logarithmic forms are closed) so that
we get a canonical splitting.
Note that
H^{j}OL_{X}
is NOT equal to
OL_{X}^{\theta_{j}}
. In fact we have for

w\in H_{pet}^{l}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}},\Omega^{j-l}_{X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}},\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits,\mathcal{O}}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})

\pi_{l}\circ\theta_{j}(X)\circ H^{j}OL_{X}(w)=H^{l}OL_{X}^{j-l}(w)

where

\pi_{l}:\oplus_{0\leq l\leq j}H_{pet}^{l}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}},\Omega^{j-l}_{X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}}(\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits D_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}))\to H_{pet}^{l}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}},\Omega^{j-l}_{X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}}(\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits D_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}))

is the projection.

3
Integral complex and
p
-adic periods of a smooth algebraic variety
over a field
k
of finite type over
\mathbb{Q}

3.1
Complex integral periods

Let
k
a field of characteristic zero.

Let
X\in\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(k)
a smooth variety. Let
X=\cup_{i=1}^{s}X_{i}
an open affine cover.
We have for
\sigma:k\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
an embedding, the evaluation period embedding map
which is the morphism of bi-complexes

\displaystyle ev(X)^{\bullet}_{\bullet}:\Gamma(X_{\bullet},\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{\bullet}})\to\mathbb{Z}\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits_{\mathop{\rm Diff}\nolimits}(\mathbb{I}^{\bullet},X^{an}_{\mathbb{C},\bullet})^{\vee}\otimes\mathbb{C},

\displaystyle w^{l}_{I}\in\Gamma(X_{I},\Omega^{l}_{X_{I}})\mapsto(ev(X)^{l}_{I}(w^{l}_{I}):\phi^{l}_{I}\in\mathbb{Z}\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits_{\mathop{\rm Diff}\nolimits}(\mathbb{I}^{l},X^{an}_{\mathbb{C},I})^{\vee}\otimes\mathbb{C}\mapsto ev^{l}_{I}(w^{l}_{I})(\phi^{l}_{I}):=\int_{\mathbb{I}^{l}}\phi_{I}^{l*}w^{l}_{I})

given by integration. By taking all the affine open cover
(j_{i}:X_{i}\hookrightarrow X)
of
X
,
we get for
\sigma:k\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
, the evaluation period embedding map

\displaystyle ev(X):=\varinjlim_{(j_{i}:X_{i}\hookrightarrow X)}ev(X)^{\bullet}_{\bullet}:\varinjlim_{(j_{i}:X_{i}\hookrightarrow X)}\Gamma(X_{\bullet},\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{\bullet}})\to\varinjlim_{(j_{i}:X_{i}\hookrightarrow X)}\mathbb{Z}\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits_{\mathop{\rm Diff}\nolimits(\mathbb{R})}(\mathbb{I}^{\bullet},X^{an}_{\mathbb{C},\bullet})^{\vee}\otimes\mathbb{C}

It induces in cohomology, for
j\in\mathbb{Z}
, the evaluation period map

\displaystyle H^{j}ev(X)=H^{j}ev(X)^{\bullet}_{\bullet}:H^{j}_{DR}(X)=H^{j}\Gamma(X_{\bullet},\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{\bullet}})\to H_{\mathop{\rm sing}\nolimits}^{j}(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an},\mathbb{C})=H^{j}(\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits_{\mathop{\rm Diff}\nolimits(\mathbb{R})}(\mathbb{I}^{\bullet},X^{an}_{\mathbb{C},\bullet})^{\vee}\otimes\mathbb{C}).

which does NOT depend on the choice of the affine open cover
by acyclicity of quasi-coherent sheaves on affine noetherian schemes for the left hand side
and from Mayer-Vietoris quasi-isomorphism for singular cohomology of topological spaces
and Whitney approximation theorem for differential manifolds for the right hand side.

Remark 1
.

We also have for
\sigma:k\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
the composition

\displaystyle\bar{e}v(X)^{\bullet}_{\bullet}:\Gamma(X_{\bullet},\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{\bullet}})\xrightarrow{ev(X)^{\bullet}_{\bullet}}\mathbb{Z}\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits_{\mathop{\rm Diff}\nolimits(\mathbb{R})}(\mathbb{I}^{\bullet},X^{an}_{\mathbb{C}\bullet})^{\vee}\otimes\mathbb{C}\xrightarrow{\mathbb{Z}(X)(i)\circ\mathop{\rm an}\nolimits^{-,-}}\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits_{\mathop{\rm Fun}\nolimits(\Delta^{\bullet},\mathop{\rm Var}\nolimits(k))}(\mathbb{D}_{k,et}^{\bullet},X_{\bullet})^{\vee}\otimes\mathbb{C}

where
i:I^{\bullet}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{D}^{\bullet}_{k,et}
is the embedding, which is given by integration :
for
w^{l}_{I}\in\Gamma(X_{I},\Omega^{l}_{X_{I}})
and
\phi^{l}_{I}\in\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits_{\mathop{\rm Fun}\nolimits(\Delta^{\bullet},\mathop{\rm Var}\nolimits(k))}(\mathbb{D}_{k,et}^{j},X_{I})
,

\bar{e}v^{l}_{I}(w^{l}_{I})(\phi^{l}_{I})=\int_{\mathbb{I}^{l}}\phi_{I}^{l,an*}w^{l}_{I}.

Let
X\in\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(k)
. Note that

H^{*}ev(X_{\mathbb{C}}):H^{*}_{DR}(X_{\mathbb{C}})\xrightarrow{H^{*}R\Gamma(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an},E_{zar}(\Omega(\mathop{\rm an}\nolimits_{X})))}H^{*}_{DR}(X^{an}_{\mathbb{C}})\xrightarrow{H^{*}R\Gamma(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an},\alpha(X))}H^{*}_{\mathop{\rm sing}\nolimits}(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an},\mathbb{C})

is the canonical isomorphism induced by the analytical functor and the quasi-isomorphism

\alpha(X):\mathbb{C}_{X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an}}\hookrightarrow\Omega^{\bullet}_{X^{an}_{\mathbb{C}}}

in
C(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an})
. Hence,

H^{*}ev(X)=H^{*}_{DR}(X)\xrightarrow{\Omega(\pi_{k/\mathbb{C}}(X))}H^{*}_{DR}(X_{\mathbb{C}})\xrightarrow{H^{*}ev(X_{\mathbb{C}})}H^{*}_{\mathop{\rm sing}\nolimits}(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an},\mathbb{C})

is injective.
The elements of the image
H^{*}ev(X)(H^{*}_{DR}(X))\subset H^{*}_{\mathop{\rm sing}\nolimits}(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an},\mathbb{C})
are the periods of
X
.

Let
X\in\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(k)
a smooth variety. Let
X=\cup_{i=1}^{s}X_{i}
an open affine cover with
X_{i}:=X\backslash D_{i}

with
D_{i}\subset X
smooth divisors with normal crossing.
Let
\sigma:k\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
an embedding and
X^{an}_{\mathbb{C}}=\cup_{i=1}^{r}\mathbb{D}_{i}

an open cover with
\mathbb{D}_{i}\simeq D(0,1)^{d}
.
Since a convex open subset of
\mathbb{C}^{d}
is biholomorphic to an open ball we have

\mathbb{D}_{I}:=\cap_{i\in I}\mathbb{D}_{i}\simeq D(0,1)^{d}
(where
d
is the dimension of a connected component of
X
).
Denote by
j_{\bullet}:X^{an}_{\bullet,\mathbb{C}}\cap\mathbb{D}_{\bullet}\to X^{an}_{\bullet,\mathbb{C}}

is the open embeddings. We then have the period morphism of tri-complexes

\displaystyle ev(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an})^{\bullet}_{\bullet,\bullet}:\Gamma(X^{an}_{\bullet,\mathbb{C}}\cap\mathbb{D}_{\bullet},\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an}})\to\mathbb{Z}\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits_{\mathop{\rm Diff}\nolimits}(\mathbb{I}^{\bullet},X^{an}_{\mathbb{C},\bullet})^{\vee}\otimes\mathbb{C},

\displaystyle w^{l}_{I,J}\in\Gamma(X_{I,\mathbb{C}}^{an}\cap\mathbb{D}_{J},\mathop{\rm an}\nolimits_{X}^{*}\Omega^{l}_{X_{I}})\mapsto

\displaystyle(ev^{l}_{I,J}(w^{l}_{I,J}):\phi^{l}_{I,J}\in\mathbb{Z}\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits_{\mathop{\rm Diff}\nolimits}(\mathbb{I}^{l},X^{an}_{\mathbb{C},I}\cap\mathbb{D}_{J})^{\vee}\otimes\mathbb{C}\mapsto ev^{l}_{I,J}(w^{l}_{I,J})(\phi^{l}_{I,J}):=\int_{\mathbb{I}^{l}}\phi_{I,J}^{l*}w^{l}_{I,J})

given by integration. We have then the factorization

\displaystyle H^{j}ev(X):H^{j}_{DR}(X):=\mathbb{H}^{j}(X,\Omega^{\bullet}_{X})=\mathbb{H}_{et}^{j}(X,\Omega^{\bullet}_{X^{et}})\xrightarrow{H^{j}\Omega(\pi_{k/\mathbb{C}}(X))}

\displaystyle H^{j}_{DR}(X_{\mathbb{C}}):=\mathbb{H}^{j}(X_{\mathbb{C}},\Omega^{\bullet}_{X})=\mathbb{H}_{et}^{j}(X_{\mathbb{C}},\Omega^{\bullet}_{X^{et}})\xrightarrow{j_{\bullet}^{*}\circ\mathop{\rm an}\nolimits_{X_{\bullet}}^{*}}H^{j}\Gamma(X^{an}_{\bullet,\mathbb{C}}\cap\mathbb{D}_{\bullet},\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an}})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{H^{j}ev(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an})^{\bullet}_{\bullet,\bullet}}H_{\mathop{\rm sing}\nolimits}^{j}(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an}\cap\mathbb{D}_{\bullet},\mathbb{C})=H^{j}(\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits_{\mathop{\rm Diff}\nolimits(\mathbb{R})}(\mathbb{I}^{\bullet},X^{an}_{\mathbb{C},\bullet}\cap\mathbb{D}_{\bullet})^{\vee}\otimes\mathbb{C}).

where for the left hand side, the first equality follows from the fact that

\Omega^{\bullet}\in C(\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(k))
is
\mathbb{A}^{1}
local and admits transferts,
and the equality of the right hand side follows
from Mayer-Vietoris quasi-isomorphism for singular cohomology of topological spaces.

Remark 2
.

Let
X\in\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(k)
a smooth variety. Let
X=\cup_{i=1}^{s}X_{i}
an open affine cover with
X_{i}:=X\backslash D_{i}

with
D_{i}\subset X
smooth divisors with normal crossing.
Let
\sigma:k\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
an embedding and
X^{an}_{\mathbb{C}}=\cup_{i=1}^{r}\mathbb{D}_{i}

an open cover with
\mathbb{D}_{i}\simeq D(0,1)^{d}
.
Since a convex open subset of
\mathbb{C}^{d}
is biholomorphic to an open ball we have

\mathbb{D}_{I}:=\cap_{i\in I}\mathbb{D}_{i}\simeq D(0,1)^{d}
(where
d
is the dimension of a connected component of
X
).
Denote by
j_{\bullet}:X^{an}_{\bullet,\mathbb{C}}\cap\mathbb{D}_{\bullet}\to X^{an}_{\bullet,\mathbb{C}}

the open embeddings. Then,

\displaystyle j_{\bullet}^{*}\circ\mathop{\rm an}\nolimits_{X_{\bullet}}^{*}:=\Omega(j_{\bullet}\circ\mathop{\rm an}\nolimits_{X_{\bullet}}):\Gamma(X_{\bullet,\mathbb{C}},\Omega^{\bullet}_{X^{et}})\to\Gamma(X^{an}_{\bullet,\mathbb{C}}\cap\mathbb{D}_{\bullet},\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an}})

is a quasi-isomorphism by the Grothendieck comparaison theorem for De Rham cohomology
and the acyclicity of quasi-coherent sheaves on noetherian affine schemes.

Lemma 1
.

Let
k\subset\mathbb{C}
a subfield.
Let
X\in\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(k)
a smooth variety. Let
(r_{i}:X_{i}\to X)_{1\leq i\leq s}
an affine etale cover.
Let
X^{an}_{\mathbb{C}}=\cup_{i=1}^{r}\mathbb{D}_{i}
an open cover with
\mathbb{D}_{i}\simeq D(0,1)^{d}
. Let

w=[\sum_{I,J,l,cardI+cardJ+l=j}w^{l}_{I,J}]=\sum_{I,J,l,cardI+cardJ+l=j}[w^{l}_{I,J}]\in H^{j}\Gamma(X^{an}_{\bullet,\mathbb{C}}\cap\mathbb{D}_{\bullet},\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an}}).

Then the following assertions are equivalent :

(i)

H^{j}ev(X)(w)\in H^{j}_{\mathop{\rm sing}\nolimits}(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an},2i\pi\mathbb{Q})
,

(ii)

for all
I,J,l
such that
cardI+cardJ+l=j
, there exist a lift

[\tilde{w}^{l}_{IJ}]\in H^{l}\Gamma(X^{an}_{I,\mathbb{C}}\cap\mathbb{D}_{J},\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an}})

of
[w^{l}_{IJ}]
with respect to the spectral sequence associated to the filtration on the total complex
associated to the bi-complex structure such that

H^{l}ev(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an})^{\bullet}_{I,J}([\tilde{w}^{l}_{I,J}])\in H^{l}_{\mathop{\rm sing}\nolimits}(X_{I,\mathbb{C}}^{an}\cap\mathbb{D}_{J},2i\pi\mathbb{Q}).

Proof.

Follows immediately form the fact that

ev(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an})^{\bullet}_{\bullet,\bullet}
define by definition a morphism of spectral sequence for the filtration
given by the bi-complex structure.
∎

Lemma 2
.

Let
k\subset\mathbb{C}
a subfield.
Let
\pi:X^{\prime}\to X
a proper generically finite (of degree
d
) morphism with
X,X^{\prime}\in\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(k)
.
Let
w\in H^{j}_{DR}(X)
. Then
ev(X)(w)\in H^{j}_{\mathop{\rm sing}\nolimits}(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an},2i\pi\mathbb{Q})
if and only if

ev(X^{\prime})(\pi^{*}w)\in H^{j}_{\mathop{\rm sing}\nolimits}(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{{}^{\prime}an},2i\pi\mathbb{Q})
.

Proof.

Since
ev(X^{\prime})(\pi^{*}w)=\pi^{*}ev(X)(w)\in H^{j}_{\mathop{\rm sing}\nolimits}(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{{}^{\prime}an},\mathbb{C})
,
the only if part is obvious whereas the if part follows from the formula of
\pi_{*}\pi^{*}=dI
.
∎

The main proposition of this section is the following :

Proposition 1
.

Let
k
a field of characteristic zero. Let
X\in\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(k)
.
Let
\sigma:k\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
an embedding.

(i)

Let
w\in H^{j}_{DR}(X):=\mathbb{H}^{j}(X,\Omega^{\bullet}_{X})=\mathbb{H}_{pet}^{j}(X,\Omega^{\bullet}_{X})
. If

w\in H^{j}OL_{X}(\mathbb{H}_{pet}^{j}(X_{\mathbb{C}},\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{\mathbb{C}}^{et},\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits}))

then
H^{j}ev(X)(w)\in H^{j}_{\mathop{\rm sing}\nolimits}(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an},2i\pi\mathbb{Q})
.

(ii)

Let
p\in\mathbb{N}
a prime number and
\sigma_{p}:k\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}_{p}
an embedding.
Let
j\in\mathbb{Z}
.
Let
w\in H^{j}_{DR}(X):=\mathbb{H}^{j}(X,\Omega^{\bullet}_{X})=\mathbb{H}_{pet}^{j}(X,\Omega^{\bullet}_{X})
. If

w:=\pi_{k/\mathbb{C}_{p}}(X)^{*}w\in H^{j}OL_{X}(\mathbb{H}_{et}^{j}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}},\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}^{et},\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits,\mathcal{O}}))

then
H^{j}ev(X)(w)\in H^{j}_{\mathop{\rm sing}\nolimits}(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an},2i\pi\mathbb{Q})
. Recall that

\mathbb{H}_{pet}^{j}(X_{\mathbb{C}},\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}^{pet},\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits,\mathcal{O}})=\mathbb{H}_{et}^{j}(X_{\mathbb{C}},\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}^{et},\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits,\mathcal{O}})
.

Proof.

(i):
Let

w\in H^{j}_{DR}(X):=\mathbb{H}^{j}(X,\Omega^{\bullet}_{X})=H^{j}\Gamma(X_{\bullet},\Omega^{\bullet}_{X}).

where
(r_{i}:X_{i}\to X)_{1\leq i\leq s}
is an affine etale cover.
Let
X^{an}_{\mathbb{C}}=\cup_{i=1}^{r}\mathbb{D}_{i}
an open cover with
\mathbb{D}_{i}\simeq D(0,1)^{d}
.
Denote
j_{IJ}:X_{I}\cap\mathbb{D}_{J}\hookrightarrow X_{I}
the open embeddings.
Then by definition
H^{j}ev(X)(w)=H^{j}ev(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an})(j_{\bullet}^{*}\circ\mathop{\rm an}\nolimits_{X_{\bullet}}^{*}w)
with

j_{\bullet}^{*}\circ\mathop{\rm an}\nolimits_{X_{\bullet}}^{*}w\in H^{j}\Gamma(X^{an}_{\bullet,\mathbb{C}}\cap\mathbb{D}_{\bullet},\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an}}).

Now, if

w=H^{j}OL_{X}(\mathbb{H}_{et}^{j}(X_{\mathbb{C}},\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{\mathbb{C}}^{et},\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits}))
,
we have a canonical splitting

\displaystyle w=\sum_{l=0}^{j}w_{L}^{l,j-l}=\sum_{l=d}^{j}w^{l,j-l}\in H^{j}_{DR}(X_{\mathbb{C}}),\;w_{L}^{l,j-l}\in H^{j-l}(X_{\mathbb{C}},\Omega^{l}_{X^{et}_{\mathbb{C}},\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits}),\;w^{l,j-l}:=H^{j}OL_{X_{\mathbb{C}}^{et}}(w_{L}^{l,j-l}).

Let
0\leq l\leq j
. Using an affine w-contractile pro-etale cover of
X
,
we see that there exists an affine etale cover

r=r(w^{l,j-l})=(r_{i}:X_{i}\to X)_{1\leq i\leq n}
of
X
(depending on
w^{l,j-l}
) such that

\displaystyle w^{l,j-l}=[(w^{l,j-l}_{L,I})_{I}]\in H^{j}OL_{X_{\mathbb{C}}^{et}}(H^{j-l}\Gamma(X_{\mathbb{C},\bullet},\Omega^{l}_{X_{\mathbb{C}},\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits}))\subset\mathbb{H}^{j}\Gamma(X_{\mathbb{C},\bullet},\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{\mathbb{C}}}).

Note that since
X
is an algebraic variety, this also follows from a comparison theorem between
Chech cohomology of etale covers and etale cohomology.
By lemma
2
, we may assume,
up to take a desingularization
\pi:X^{\prime}\to X
of
(X,\cup_{i}(r_{i}(X\backslash X_{i})))
and replace
w
with
\pi^{*}w
,
that
r_{i}(X_{i})=r_{i}(X_{i}(w))=X\backslash D_{i}
with
D_{i}\subset X
smooth divisors with normal crossing
For
1\leq l\leq j
, we get

\displaystyle w^{l,j-l}_{L,I}=\sum_{\nu}df_{\nu_{1}}/f_{\nu_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge df_{\nu_{l}}/f_{\nu_{l}}\in\Gamma(X_{\mathbb{C},I},\Omega^{l}_{X_{\mathbb{C}}}).

For
l=0
, we get

w^{0,j}=[(\lambda_{I})]\in H^{j}\Gamma(X_{\mathbb{C},I},O_{X_{\mathbb{C}_{p},I}}),\;\lambda_{I}\in\Gamma(X_{\mathbb{C},I},\mathbb{Z}_{X_{\mathbb{C},I}^{et}})

There exists
k^{\prime}\subset\mathbb{C}
containing
k
such that
w^{l,j-l}_{L,I}\in\Gamma(X_{k^{\prime},I},\Omega^{l}_{X_{k^{\prime}}})

for all
0\leq l\leq j
.
Taking an embedding
\sigma^{\prime}:k^{\prime}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
such that
\sigma^{\prime}_{|k}=\sigma
, we then have

\displaystyle j_{\bullet}^{*}\circ\mathop{\rm an}\nolimits_{X_{\bullet}}^{*}w=j_{\bullet}^{*}((m_{l}\cdot w^{l,j-l}_{L})_{0\leq l\leq j})=(w^{l,j-l}_{L,I,J})_{l,I,J}\in H^{j}\Gamma(X^{an}_{\bullet,\mathbb{C}}\cap\mathbb{D}_{\bullet},\Omega_{X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an}}^{\bullet}).

where for each
(I,J,l)
with
cardI+cardJ+l=j
,

w^{l,j-l}_{L,I,J}:=j_{IJ}^{*}w_{L,I}^{l,j-l}\in\Gamma(X^{an}_{I,\mathbb{C}}\cap\mathbb{D}_{J},\Omega_{X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an}}^{l}).

We have by a standard computation, for each
(I,J,l)
with
cardI+cardJ+l=j
,

H^{*}_{\mathop{\rm sing}\nolimits}(X_{I,\mathbb{C}}^{an}\cap\mathbb{D}_{J},\mathbb{Z})=<\gamma_{1},\cdots,\gamma_{cardI}>,

where for
1\leq i\leq cardI
,
\gamma_{i}\in\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits(\Delta^{*},X_{I,\mathbb{C}}^{an}\cap\mathbb{D}_{J})

are products of loops around the origin inside the pointed disc
\mathbb{D}^{1}\backslash 0
.
On the other hand,

•

w^{l,j-l}_{L,I,J}=j_{J}^{*}(\sum_{\nu}df_{\nu_{1}}/f_{\nu_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge df_{\nu_{l}}/f_{\nu_{l}})\in\Gamma(X_{I,\mathbb{C}}^{an}\cap\mathbb{D}_{J},\Omega^{l}_{X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an}})

for
1\leq l\leq j
,

•

w^{0,j}_{L,I,J}=\lambda_{I}
is a constant.

Hence, for
\mu\in P([1,\cdots,s])
with
card\mu=l
, we get, for
l=0

H^{l}ev(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an})_{I,J}(w_{L,I,J}^{0,j})=0
and, for
1\leq l\leq j
,

\displaystyle H^{l}ev(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an})_{I,J}(w_{L,I,J}^{l,j-l})(\gamma_{\mu})=\sum_{k}\delta_{\nu,\mu}2i\pi\in 2i\pi\mathbb{Z}.

where
\gamma_{\mu}:=\gamma_{\mu_{1}}\cdots\gamma_{\mu_{l}}
.
We conclude by lemma
1
.

(ii):It is a particular case of (i).
∎

Let
k
a field of characteristic zero.
Let
X\in\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(k)
. Let
X=\cup_{i=1}^{s}X_{i}
an open affine cover with
X_{i}:=X\backslash D_{i}

with
D_{i}\subset X
smooth divisors with normal crossing. Let
\sigma:k\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
an embedding.
By proposition
1
, we have a commutative diagram of graded algebras

\textstyle{H^{*}_{DR}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{H^{*}ev(X)}

\textstyle{H^{*}_{\mathop{\rm sing}\nolimits}(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an},\mathbb{C})}
\textstyle{H^{*}OL_{X}(\mathbb{H}_{et}^{*}(X,\Omega^{\bullet}_{X^{et},\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits}))\cap H^{*}_{DR}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\subset}
\scriptstyle{H^{*}ev(X)}

\textstyle{H^{*}_{\mathop{\rm sing}\nolimits}(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an},2i\pi\mathbb{Q})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{H^{*}C^{*}\iota_{2i\pi\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{C}}(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an})}

where

C^{*}\iota_{2i\pi\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{C}}(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an}):C^{\bullet}_{\mathop{\rm sing}\nolimits}(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an},2i\pi\mathbb{Q})\hookrightarrow C^{\bullet}_{\mathop{\rm sing}\nolimits}(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an},\mathbb{C})

is the subcomplex consiting of
\alpha\in C^{j}_{\mathop{\rm sing}\nolimits}(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an},\mathbb{C})

such that
\alpha(\gamma)\in 2i\pi\mathbb{Q}
for all
\gamma\in C_{j}^{\mathop{\rm sing}\nolimits}(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an},\mathbb{Q})
.
Recall that

H^{*}ev(X_{\mathbb{C}})=H^{*}R\Gamma(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an},\alpha(X))\circ\Gamma(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an},E_{zar}(\Omega(\mathop{\rm an}\nolimits_{X}))):H^{*}_{DR}(X_{\mathbb{C}})\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{*}_{\mathop{\rm sing}\nolimits}(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an},\mathbb{C})

is the canonical isomorphism induced by the analytical functor and

\alpha(X):\mathbb{C}_{X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an}}\hookrightarrow\Omega^{\bullet}_{X^{an}_{\mathbb{C}}}
,
which gives the periods elements
H^{*}ev(X)(H^{*}_{DR}(X))\subset H^{*}_{\mathop{\rm sing}\nolimits}(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an},\mathbb{C})
.
On the other side the induced map

H^{*}ev(X_{\mathbb{C}}):H^{*}OL_{X}(\mathbb{H}_{et}^{*}(X_{\mathbb{C}},\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{\mathbb{C}}^{et},\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits}))\hookrightarrow H^{*}\iota_{2i\pi\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{C}}H^{*}_{\mathop{\rm sing}\nolimits}(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an},2i\pi\mathbb{Q})

is NOT surjective in general since the left hand side is invariant by the action of the group

Aut(\mathbb{C})
(the group of field automorphism of
\mathbb{C}
)
whereas the right hand side is not.
The fact for a de Rham cohomology class of being logarithmic is algebraic
and invariant under isomorphism of (abstract) schemes.

3.2
Rigid GAGA for logarithmic de Rham classes

Let
K
a field of characteristic zero which is complete for a
p
-adic norm.
We consider
\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits^{int}/O_{K}:=O(\mathop{\rm PSch}\nolimits^{2}/O_{K})\subset\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits^{ft}/O_{K}
the full subcategory
consisting of integrable models of algebraic varieties over
K
,
where
O:\mathop{\rm PSch}\nolimits^{2}/O_{K}\to\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits^{ft}/O_{K},\;O(X,Z)=X\backslash Z
is the canonical functor.
Denote by
\mathop{\rm Var}\nolimits(K)^{pet}
and
(\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits^{int}/O_{K})^{pet}
the big pro-etale sites.
We then have the morphsim of sites
r:\mathop{\rm Var}\nolimits(K)^{pet}\to(\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits^{int}/O_{K})^{pet}

We will consider

\Omega^{\bullet,an}_{/K,\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits,\mathcal{O}}\in C((\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits^{int}/O_{K})^{pet}),\;X^{\mathcal{O}}\mapsto\Omega^{\bullet}_{\hat{X}^{(p)},\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits,\mathcal{O}}(\hat{X}^{\mathcal{O},(p)}),\,(f:X^{\prime}\to X)\mapsto\Omega(f):=f^{*}

and the embedding of
C(\mathop{\rm Var}\nolimits(K)^{pet})
(see definition
1
(iii))

\displaystyle OL:=OL_{/K,an}:r^{*}\Omega^{\bullet,an}_{/K,\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits,\mathcal{O}}\hookrightarrow\Omega_{/K}^{\bullet,an},\;\mbox{for}\,X\in\mathop{\rm Var}\nolimits(K)^{pet},

\displaystyle OL_{/K,an}(X):=OL_{\hat{X}^{(p)}}(X):\varinjlim_{X^{\mathcal{O}}\in(\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits^{int}/O_{K})^{pet},X^{\mathcal{O}}\times_{O_{K}}K=X}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\hat{X}^{(p)},\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits,\mathcal{O}}(\hat{X}^{(p)})\to\Omega^{\bullet}_{\hat{X}^{(p)}}(\hat{X}^{(p)}).

and their restrictions to
\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(K)^{pet}\subset\mathop{\rm Var}\nolimits(K)^{pet}
.
Denote
\mathop{\rm FSch}\nolimits/O_{K}
is the category of formal schemes over
O_{K}
.
The morphism of site in
\mathop{\rm RCat}\nolimits
given by completion with respect to
(p)

c:\mathop{\rm FSch}\nolimits/O_{K}\to\mathop{\rm Var}\nolimits(K),\;X\mapsto\hat{X}:=\hat{X}^{\mathcal{O},(p)}\otimes_{O_{K}}K,\;X^{\mathcal{O}}\in\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits^{int}/O_{K},\,\mbox{s.t}\,X^{\mathcal{O}}\otimes_{O_{K}}K=X

induces the map in
C(\mathop{\rm Var}\nolimits(K)^{pet})

c^{*}:\Omega^{\bullet}_{/K}\to\Omega_{/K}^{\bullet,an}:=c_{*}\Omega_{/K}^{\bullet,an},\;\mbox{for}\,X\in\mathop{\rm Var}\nolimits(K),\,c^{*}:\Omega^{\bullet}_{X}(X)\to\Omega^{\bullet}_{\hat{X}^{(p)}}(\hat{X}^{(p)}),

which induces for each
j\in\mathbb{Z}
and
X\in\mathop{\rm Var}\nolimits(K)
the morphism

c^{*}:H^{j}_{DR}(X)=\mathbb{H}_{et}^{j}(X,\Omega^{\bullet}_{X})\to\mathbb{H}_{et}^{j}(X,\Omega^{\bullet}_{\hat{X}^{(p)}})=\mathbb{H}_{et}^{j}(\hat{X}^{(p)},\Omega^{\bullet}_{\hat{X}^{(p)}})

which is an isomorphism for
X\in\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(K)
or
X\in\mathop{\rm PVar}\nolimits(K)
by GAGA (c.f. EGA 3) and the fact that
for the etale toplogy
c_{*}=Rc_{*}
.
On the on the other hand, it is well-known (see
[
20
]
) that for
X\in\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(K)
, by taking an open cover
X=\cup_{i}X_{i}

such that there exists etale maps
X_{i}\to T^{d_{X}}\subset\mathbb{A}^{d_{X}}
, we have

\mathbb{H}_{pet}^{j}(\hat{X}^{(p)},\Omega^{\bullet}_{\hat{X}^{(p)}})=\mathbb{H}_{et}^{j}(\hat{X}^{(p)},\Omega^{\bullet}_{\hat{X}^{(p)}}),\;\mbox{that is},\;\mathbb{H}_{pet}^{j}(X,\Omega^{\bullet}_{\hat{X}^{(p)}})=\mathbb{H}_{et}^{j}(X,\Omega^{\bullet}_{\hat{X}^{(p)}}).

We have also the sheaves

\displaystyle\hat{O},\hat{O}^{*}\in\mathop{\rm PSh}\nolimits(\mathop{\rm Var}\nolimits(K)),\;X\in\mathop{\rm Var}\nolimits(K)\mapsto\hat{O}(X):=\varprojlim_{n\in\mathbb{N}}O(X^{\mathcal{O}}_{/p^{n}}),\,\hat{O}^{*}(X):=\varprojlim_{n\in\mathbb{N}}O(X^{\mathcal{O}}_{/p^{n}})^{*},

\displaystyle(g:Y\to X)\mapsto(a_{g}(X^{\mathcal{O}}_{/p^{n}}))_{n\in\mathbb{N}}:\hat{O}(X)\to\hat{O}(Y),\,\hat{O}(X)^{*}\to\hat{O}(Y)^{*}

The sheaf
\hat{O}^{*}\in\mathop{\rm PSh}\nolimits(\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(k))
admits transfers : for
W\subset X^{\prime}\times X
with
X,X^{\prime}\in\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(K)

and
W
finite over
X^{\prime}
and
f\in O(X^{\mathcal{O}}_{/p^{n}})^{*}
,
W^{*}f:=N_{W/X^{\prime}}(p_{X}^{*}f)

where
p_{X}:W\hookrightarrow X^{\prime}\times X\to X
is the projection and
N_{W/X^{\prime}}:k(W)^{*}\to k(X^{\prime})^{*}
is the norm map.
This gives transfers on
\Omega^{1,an}_{/K,\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits,\mathcal{O}}\in\mathop{\rm PSh}\nolimits(\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(K))
compatible with transfers on

\Omega^{1,an}_{/K}\in\mathop{\rm PSh}\nolimits(\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(K))
:
for
W\subset X^{\prime}\times X
with
X,X^{\prime}\in\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(k)
and
W
finite over
X^{\prime}
and
f\in O(X^{\mathcal{O}}_{/p^{n}})^{*}
,

W^{*}df/f:=dW^{*}f/W^{*}f=Tr_{W/X^{\prime}}(p_{X}^{*}(df/f)),

where where
p_{X}:W\hookrightarrow X^{\prime}\times X\to X
is the projection and
Tr_{W/X^{\prime}}:O_{W}\to O_{X}
is the trace map.
Note that
d(fg)/fg=df/f+dg/g
. We get transfers on

\otimes^{l}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\Omega^{1,an}_{/K,\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits,\mathcal{O}},\,\otimes_{\hat{O}}^{l}\Omega^{1,an}_{/K}\in\mathop{\rm PSh}\nolimits(\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(K))

since

\otimes^{l}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\Omega^{1,an}_{/K,\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits,\mathcal{O}}=H^{0}(\otimes^{L,l}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\Omega^{1,an}_{/K,\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits,\mathcal{O}})

and
\otimes_{\hat{O}}^{l}\Omega^{1,an}_{/k}=H^{0}(\otimes_{\hat{O}}^{L,l}\Omega^{1,an}_{/K})
.
This induces transfers on

\displaystyle\wedge^{l}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\Omega^{1,an}_{/K,\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits,\mathcal{O}}:=\mathop{\rm coker}\nolimits(\oplus_{I_{2}\subset[1,\ldots,l]}\otimes^{l-1}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\Omega^{1,an}_{/K,\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits,\mathcal{O}}\xrightarrow{\oplus_{I_{2}\subset[1,\ldots,l]}\Delta_{I_{2}}:=(w\otimes w^{\prime}\mapsto w\otimes w\otimes w^{\prime})}\otimes^{l}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\Omega^{1,an}_{/K,\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits,\mathcal{O}})

\displaystyle\in\mathop{\rm PSh}\nolimits(\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(K)).

and

\wedge^{l}_{\hat{O}}\Omega^{1,an}_{/K}:=\mathop{\rm coker}\nolimits(\oplus_{I_{2}\subset[1,\ldots,l]}\otimes^{l-1}_{\hat{O}}\Omega^{1,an}_{/K}\xrightarrow{\oplus_{I_{2}\subset[1,\ldots,l]}\Delta_{I_{2}}:=(w\otimes w^{\prime}\mapsto w\otimes w\otimes w^{\prime})})\otimes^{l}_{\hat{O}}\Omega^{1,an}_{/K}\in\mathop{\rm PSh}\nolimits(\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(K)).

We will use the following standard constructions and facts on closed pairs of algebraic varieties
(see
[
9
]
) :

•

Let
(X,Z)\in\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits^{2}
with
X\in\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits
a noetherian scheme and
Z\subset X
a closed subset.
We have the deformation
(D_{Z}X,\mathbb{A}^{1}_{Z})\to\mathbb{A}^{1}
,
(D_{Z}X,\mathbb{A}^{1}_{Z})\in\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits^{2}

of
(X,Z)
by the normal cone
C_{Z/X}\to Z
, i.e. such that

(D_{Z}X,\mathbb{A}^{1}_{Z})_{s}=(X,Z),\,s\in\mathbb{A}^{1}\backslash 0,\;(D_{Z}X,\mathbb{A}^{1}_{Z})_{0}=(C_{Z/X},Z).

We denote by
i_{1}:(X,Z)\hookrightarrow(D_{Z}X,\mathbb{A}^{1}_{Z})
and

i_{0}:(C_{Z/X},Z)\hookrightarrow(D_{Z}X,\mathbb{A}^{1}_{Z})
the closed embeddings in
\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits^{2}
.

•

Let
k
a field of characteristic zero. Let
X\in\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(k)
.
For
Z\subset X
a closed subset of pure codimension
c
,
consider a desingularisation
\epsilon:\tilde{Z}\to Z
of
Z
and denote
n:\tilde{Z}\xrightarrow{\epsilon}Z\subset X
.
We have then the morphism in
\mathop{\rm DA}\nolimits(k)

G_{Z,X}:M(X)\xrightarrow{D(\mathbb{Z}(n))}M(\tilde{Z})(c)[2c]\xrightarrow{\mathbb{Z}(\epsilon)}M(Z)(c)[2c]

where
D:\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits_{\mathop{\rm DA}\nolimits(k)}(M_{c}(\tilde{Z}),M_{c}(X))\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits_{\mathop{\rm DA}\nolimits(k)}(M(X),M(\tilde{Z})(c)[2c])

is the duality isomorphism from the six functors formalism (moving lemma of Suzlin and Voevodsky)
and
\mathbb{Z}(n):=\mathop{\rm ad}\nolimits(n_{!},n^{!})(a_{X}^{!}\mathbb{Z})
, noting that
n_{!}=n_{*}
since
n
is proper and that

a_{X}^{!}=a_{X}^{*}[d_{X}]
and
a_{\tilde{Z}}^{!}=a_{\tilde{Z}}^{*}[d_{Z}]
since
X
, resp.
\tilde{Z}
, are smooth
(considering the connected components, we may assume
X
and
\tilde{Z}
of pure dimension).

•

Let
X\in\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(k)
and
Z\subset X
a smooth closed subvariety.
The closed embeddings
i_{1}:(X,Z)\hookrightarrow(D_{Z}X,\mathbb{A}^{1}_{Z})
and

i_{0}:(C_{Z/X},Z)\hookrightarrow(D_{Z}X,\mathbb{A}^{1}_{Z})
in
\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits^{2}(k)
induces isomorphisms of motives

\mathbb{Z}(i_{1}):M_{Z}(X)\xrightarrow{\sim}M_{\mathbb{A}^{1}_{Z}}(D_{Z}X)
and

\mathbb{Z}(i_{0}):M_{Z}(N_{Z/X})\xrightarrow{\sim}M_{\mathbb{A}^{1}_{Z}}(D_{Z}X)
in
\mathop{\rm DA}\nolimits(k)
.
We get the excision isomorphism in
\mathop{\rm DA}\nolimits(k)

P_{Z,X}:=\mathbb{Z}(i_{0})^{-1}\circ\mathbb{Z}(i_{1}):M_{Z}(X)\xrightarrow{\sim}M_{Z}(N_{Z/X}).

We have

Th(N_{Z/X})\circ P_{Z,X}\circ\gamma^{\vee}_{Z}(\mathbb{Z}_{X})=G_{Z,X}:=D(\mathbb{Z}(i)):M(X)\to M(Z)(d)[2d].

The result of this section is the following :

Proposition 2
.

Let
K
a field of characteristic zero which is complete for a
p
-adic norm.

(i)

Let
X\in\mathop{\rm PSmVar}\nolimits(K)
.
For each
j,l\in\mathbb{Z}
, the isomorphism

c^{*}:\mathbb{H}_{pet}^{j}(X,\Omega^{\bullet}_{X})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{H}_{pet}^{j}(X,\Omega^{\bullet}_{\hat{X}^{(p)}})

and its inverse preserve logarithmic classes, that is

c^{*}(H^{j}(m\circ(OL_{X}\otimes I))(H_{pet}^{j-l}(X,\Omega^{l}_{X^{pet},\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits,\mathcal{O}}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})))=H^{j}OL_{\hat{X}^{(p)}}(H_{pet}^{j-l}(X,\Omega^{l}_{\hat{X}^{(p)},\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits,\mathcal{O}}))

is an isomorphism.

(ii)

Let
X\in\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(K)
.
For each
j,l\in\mathbb{Z}
, the isomorphism

c^{*}:\mathbb{H}_{pet}^{j}(X,\Omega^{\bullet}_{X})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{H}_{pet}^{j}(X,\Omega^{\bullet}_{\hat{X}^{(p)}})

and its inverse preserve logarithmic classes, that is

c^{*}(H^{j}(m\circ(OL_{X}\otimes I))(H_{pet}^{j-l}(X,\Omega^{l}_{X^{pet},\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits,\mathcal{O}}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})))=H^{j}OL_{\hat{X}^{(p)}}(H_{pet}^{j-l}(X,\Omega^{l}_{\hat{X}^{(p)},\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits,\mathcal{O}}))

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

(i):Consider, for
j,l\in\mathbb{Z}
, the presheaf

L^{l,j-l}:=H^{j}OL_{/K,an}(H^{j-l}E^{\bullet}_{pet}\Omega_{/K,\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits,\mathcal{O}}^{l,an})\in\mathop{\rm PSh}\nolimits(\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(K)).

By definition, for
X\in\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(K)
,

L^{l,j-l}(X):=H^{j}OL_{/K,an}(H^{j-l}\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}(X),E^{\bullet}_{pet}(r^{*}\Omega_{/K,\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits,\mathcal{O}}^{l,an})))=H^{j}OL_{\hat{X}^{(p)}}(H_{pet}^{j-l}(X,\Omega^{l}_{\hat{X}^{(p)},\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits,\mathcal{O}})).

Consider also for
X\in\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(K)
and
Z\subset X
a closed subset,

L_{Z}^{l,j-l}(X):=H^{j}OL_{/K,an}(H^{j-l}\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}(X,X\backslash Z),E^{\bullet}_{pet}(r^{*}\Omega_{/K,\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits,\mathcal{O}}^{l,an})))=H^{j}OL_{\hat{X}^{(p)}}(H_{pet,Z}^{j-l}(X,\Omega_{\hat{X}^{(p)},\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits,\mathcal{O}}^{l})).

For the second equalities of
L^{l,j-l}(X)
and
L_{Z}^{l,j-l}(X)
, note that

a_{et}OL_{/K,an}(r^{*}\Omega_{/K,\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits,\mathcal{O}}^{l,an})_{|X^{pet}}=a_{et}OL_{\hat{X}^{(p)}}(r^{*}\Omega_{\hat{X}^{(p)},\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits,\mathcal{O}}),

where
X^{pet}\subset\mathop{\rm Var}\nolimits(K)^{pet}
is the small pro etale site and
a_{et}:\mathop{\rm PSh}\nolimits(X^{pet})\to\mathop{\rm Shv}\nolimits_{et}(X^{pet})

is the sheaftification functor.
For
l,n\in\mathbb{N}
, consider the presheaves in
(\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits^{int}/O_{K})^{pet}

\displaystyle OL_{/O_{K},p^{n}}:\Omega^{l}_{/(O_{K}/p^{n}),\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits}\hookrightarrow\Omega^{l}_{/(O_{K}/p^{n})},\;\mbox{for}\,X^{\mathcal{O}}\in(\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits^{int}/O_{K})^{pet},

\displaystyle\Omega^{l}_{/(O_{K}/p^{n}),\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits}(X^{\mathcal{O}}):=\Omega_{X^{\mathcal{O}}/p^{n},\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits}(X^{\mathcal{O}})\hookrightarrow\Omega^{l}_{/(O_{K}/p^{n})}(X^{\mathcal{O}}):=\Omega_{X^{\mathcal{O}}/p^{n}}(X^{\mathcal{O}})

which induces the presheaves in
\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(K)^{pet}

OL_{/(O_{K}/p^{n})}:=r^{*}OL_{/O_{K},p^{n}}:r^{*}\Omega^{l}_{/(O_{K}/p^{n}),\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits}\hookrightarrow r^{*}\Omega^{l}_{/(O_{K}/p^{n})}.

Note that by definition
\Omega^{l}_{/(O_{K}/p^{n}),\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits}=\nu^{*}\nu_{*}\Omega^{l}_{/(O_{K}/p^{n}),\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits}
and

\Omega^{l}_{/(O_{K}/p^{n})}=\nu^{*}\nu_{*}\Omega^{l}_{/(O_{K}/p^{n})}
, where
\nu:(\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits^{int}/O_{K})^{pet}\to\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits^{int}/O_{K}
.
We have then for
X\in\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(K)

\displaystyle L^{l,j-l}(X)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle H^{j}OL_{\hat{X}^{(p)}}(\varprojlim_{n\in\mathbb{N}}H_{et}^{j-l}(X,\Omega^{l}_{X^{\mathcal{O}}/p^{n},\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits,\mathcal{O}}))

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle H^{j}OL_{/K,an}(\varprojlim_{n\in\mathbb{N}}H^{j-l}\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}(X,X\backslash Z),E^{\bullet}_{et}(r^{*}\Omega^{l}_{/(O_{K}/p^{n}),\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits}))),

since

R\varprojlim_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\Omega^{l}_{X^{\mathcal{O}}/p^{n},\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits,\mathcal{O}}\simeq\varprojlim_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\Omega^{l}_{X^{\mathcal{O}}/p^{n},\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits,\mathcal{O}}\in D(X^{pet})

as the map in
\mathop{\rm PSh}\nolimits(X^{pet})

r^{*}\Omega(/p^{n^{\prime}}):r^{*}\Omega^{l}_{X^{\mathcal{O}}/p^{n-n^{\prime}},\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits,\mathcal{O}}\to r^{*}\Omega^{l}_{X^{\mathcal{O}}/p^{n},\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits,\mathcal{O}},

is surjective for the etale topology, where
/p^{n^{\prime}}:X^{\mathcal{O}}/p^{n}\hookrightarrow X^{\mathcal{O}}/p^{n-n^{\prime}}

is induced by the quotient map
/p^{n^{\prime}}:O_{X^{\mathcal{O}}}/p^{n-n^{\prime}}\to O_{X^{\mathcal{O}}}/p^{n}
,
and as the pro-etale site is a replete topos (c.f.
[
2
]
).
We have for
X\in\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(K)
and
Z\subset X
a closed subset,

\displaystyle L_{Z}^{l,j-l}(X)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle H^{j}OL_{\hat{X}^{(p)}}(\varprojlim_{n\in\mathbb{N}}H_{et,Z}^{j-l}(X,\Omega^{l}_{X^{\mathcal{O}}/p^{n},\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits,\mathcal{O}}))

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle H^{j}OL_{/K,an}(\varprojlim_{n\in\mathbb{N}}H^{j-l}\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}(X,X\backslash Z),E^{\bullet}_{et}(r^{*}\Omega^{l}_{/(O_{K}/p^{n}),\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits}))).

The presheaves
\Omega^{l}_{/(O_{K}/p^{n}),\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits}\in\mathop{\rm PSh}\nolimits(\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(K))
,
l,n\in\mathbb{N}
,
are
\mathbb{A}^{1}
invariant and admit transfers.
Hence by a theorem of Voevodsky (c.f.
[
9
]
for example),

\Omega^{l}_{/(O_{K}/p^{n}),\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits}\in\mathop{\rm PSh}\nolimits(\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(K))
,
l,n\in\mathbb{N}
,
are
\mathbb{A}^{1}
local since they are
\mathbb{A}^{1}
invariant and admit transfers.
This gives in particular, for
Z\subset X
a smooth subvariety of (pure) codimension
d
, an isomorphism

\displaystyle(\Omega^{l}_{/(O_{K}/p^{n}),\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits}(P_{Z,X}))_{n\in\mathbb{N}}:L_{Z}^{l,j-l}(X)\xrightarrow{\sim}L_{Z}^{l,j-l}(N_{Z/X})\xrightarrow{\sim}L^{l-d,j-l-d}(Z).

Let

\displaystyle\alpha=OL_{\hat{X}^{(p)}}(\alpha)\in L^{l,j-l}(X):=H^{j}OL_{\hat{X}^{(p)}}(H_{pet}^{j-l}(X,\Omega^{l}_{\hat{X}^{(p)},\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits,\mathcal{O}}))=H^{j-l}\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits(\mathbb{Z}(X),E^{\bullet}_{pet}(r^{*}\Omega_{/K,\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits,\mathcal{O}}^{l,an})).

Let
U\subset X
be an affine open subset such that there exists an etale map
e:U\to T^{d_{X}}\subset\mathbb{A}^{d_{X}}
.
Let
r=(r_{i}):Y:=\varprojlim_{i\in I}U_{i}\to U
be a faithfully flat pro-etale map with
Y
w-contractile.
In particular
\bar{r}_{i}(Y^{(}0))=U
, where
Y^{(0)}\subset Y
are the closed points.
As
\Omega_{/K,\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits,\mathcal{O}}^{l,an}
consists of single presheaf, we have

r^{*}\alpha=0\in H^{j}_{DR}(\hat{Y}^{(p)})=H^{j}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\hat{X}^{(p)}}(Y)=\mathbb{H}_{pet}^{j}(Y,\Omega^{\bullet}_{\hat{X}^{(p)}}),

that is

r^{*}\alpha=0=[\partial(\eta_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}]=[(\partial\eta_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}]\in H^{j}_{DR}(\hat{Y}^{(p)}),\,\mbox{with}\,(\eta_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\in\Omega^{j+1}_{\hat{X}^{(p)}}(Y).

Denote
j:U\hookrightarrow X
the open embedding. Consider

j^{*}\alpha=[(w_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}]\in H^{j}_{DR}(\hat{U}^{(p)})=H^{j}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\hat{X}^{(p)}}(U)=\mathbb{H}_{pet}^{j}(U,\Omega^{\bullet}_{\hat{X}^{(p)}}).

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
. There exists
i_{n}\in I
(depending on
n
) such that
\eta_{n}=r_{i_{n}}^{*}\tilde{\eta}_{n}
,
with
\tilde{\eta}_{n}\in\Omega^{j+1}_{\hat{X}^{(p)}}(U_{i_{n}})
.
Then, there exists
a_{ij}:U_{j_{n}}\to U_{i_{n}}
,
j_{n},a_{ij}\in I
such that

r_{j_{n}}^{*}w_{n}=a_{ij}^{*}\partial\tilde{\eta}_{n}+\partial\beta_{n}\in\Omega^{\bullet}_{X^{\mathcal{O}}/p^{n}}(U_{j_{n}})

Since
r_{j_{n}}:U_{j_{n}}\to U
is faithfully flat, we get

w_{n}=\partial(\tilde{\eta}_{n}+\beta_{n})\in\Omega^{\bullet}_{X^{\mathcal{O}}/p^{n}}(U).

Hence
j^{*}\alpha=0\in L^{l,j-l}(U)
.
Let
r_{\bullet}:Y_{\bullet}\to U
be a pro-etale cover by
w
-contractile schemes. Then,

j^{*}\alpha=(\theta_{I})\in H_{pet}^{j-l}(U,\Omega^{l}_{\hat{X}^{(p)},\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits,\mathcal{O}})=H^{j-l}\Gamma(Y_{\bullet},\Omega^{l}_{\hat{X}^{(p)},\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits,\mathcal{O}}).

Since
j^{*}\alpha=0\in L^{l,j-l}(U)
, we have

\theta_{I}=\tilde{\theta}_{I^{\prime}|Y_{I}}+\partial\gamma_{I}\in\Gamma(Y_{I},\Omega^{l}_{\hat{X}^{(p)}}),

with
I\subset I^{\prime}
,
\tilde{\theta}_{I^{\prime}}\in\Gamma(Y_{I^{\prime}},\Omega^{l}_{\hat{X}^{(p)}})
and

\gamma_{I}\in\Gamma(Y_{I},\Omega^{l+1}_{\hat{X}^{(p)}})
.
Since
\Omega_{U}
is a trivial vector bundle as
U\to\mathbb{A}^{d_{X}}
is etale,
and since a logarithmic form is exact if and only if it vanishes, we get
\theta_{I}=\tilde{\theta}_{I^{\prime}|Y_{I}}
and

j^{*}\alpha=\partial_{\bullet}(\tilde{\theta}_{I^{\prime}})=0\in H_{pet}^{j-l}(U,\Omega^{l}_{\hat{X}^{(p)},\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits,\mathcal{O}})=H^{j-l}\Gamma(Y_{\bullet},\Omega^{l}_{\hat{X}^{(p)},\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits,\mathcal{O}}).

Considering a divisor
X\backslash U\subset D\subset X
, we get

\alpha=H^{j-l}E^{\bullet}_{pet}(r^{*}\Omega_{/K,\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits,\mathcal{O}}^{l,an})(\gamma^{\vee}_{D})(\alpha),\,\alpha\in L_{D}^{l,j-l}(X).

We then get by induction (restricting to the smooth locus of the divisors)
a closed subset
Z\subset X
of pure codimension
c=min(l,j-l)
such that

\alpha=H^{j-l}E^{\bullet}_{pet}(r^{*}\Omega_{/K,\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits,\mathcal{O}}^{l,an})(\gamma^{\vee}_{Z})(\alpha),\,\alpha\in L_{Z}^{l,j-l}(X).

Hence, we get

•

if
j\neq 2l
,
\alpha=0
, we use the fact that
X
is projective for
j<2l
,

•

if
j=2l
,
\alpha\in\oplus_{1\leq t\leq s}\mathbb{Z}_{p}[Z_{i}]
,
where
(Z_{i})_{1\leq i\leq t}\subset Z
are the irreducible components of
Z
.

(ii): Take a compactification
\bar{X}\in\mathop{\rm PSmVar}\nolimits(K)
of
X
with

\bar{X}\backslash X=\cup_{1\leq i\leq s}D_{i}\subset\bar{X}
a normal crossing divisor.
Then (ii) follows from (i) applied to
\bar{X}
and
D_{I}:=\cap_{i\in I}D_{i}
for
I\subset[1,\ldots,s]

by the distinguish triangle in
\mathop{\rm DA}\nolimits(K)

M(X)\to M(\bar{X})\to\oplus_{1\leq i\leq s}M(D_{i})(-1)[-2]\to\cdots\to M(D_{[1,\cdots,s]})(-s)[-2s].

∎

3.3
p
adic integral periods

For
k
a field of finite type over
\mathbb{Q}
and
X\in\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(k)
,
we denote
\delta(k,X)\subset N
the finite set consisting of prime numbers
such that if
p\in\mathbb{N}\backslash\delta(k,X)
is a prime number,
k
is unramified at
p
and
there exists an integral model
X^{\mathcal{O}}_{\hat{k}_{\sigma_{p}}}\in\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits^{int}/O_{\hat{k}_{\sigma_{p}}}

of
X_{\hat{k}_{\sigma_{p}}}
with good reduction modulo
p
for all embeddings
\sigma_{p}:k\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}_{p}
,

\hat{k}_{\sigma_{p}}\subset\mathbb{C}_{p}
being the
p
-adic completion of
k
with respect to
\sigma_{p}
.

Let
k
a field of finite type over
\mathbb{Q}
.
Denote
\bar{k}
the algebraic closure of
k
and

the absolute Galois group of

.

Definition 2
.

We recall the following result from Illusie:

Proposition 3
.

Let
k
a field of finite type over
\mathbb{Q}
. Let
X\in\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(k)
.
Let
p\in\mathbb{N}\backslash\delta(k,X)
a prime number. Consider an embedding
\sigma_{p}:k\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}_{p}
.

We have the following key proposition :

Proposition 4
.

Let
k
a field of finite type over
\mathbb{Q}
. Let

.

\sigma_{p}:k\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}_{p}

(i)

\displaystyle\subset H^{j}_{DR}(X_{\hat{k}_{\sigma_{p}}})\otimes_{\hat{k}_{\sigma_{p}}}\mathbb{B}_{st,\hat{k}_{\sigma_{p}}},

is exact for the pro-etale topology.

(ii)’: By (ii), if

Proposition 5
.

Let
k
a field of finite type over
\mathbb{Q}
. Denote
\bar{k}
the algebraic closure of
k
.
Denote
G:=Gal(\bar{k}/k)
its absolute Galois group.
Let
X\in\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(k)
a smooth variety.
Take a compactification
\bar{X}\in\mathop{\rm PSmVar}\nolimits(k)
of
X
such that
D:=\bar{X}\backslash X\subset X
is a normal crossing divisor,
and denote
j:X\hookrightarrow\bar{X}
the open embedding.
Let
p\in\mathbb{N}
a prime number. Consider an embedding
\sigma_{p}:k\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}_{p}
.
Denote
k\subset\hat{k}_{\sigma_{p}}\subset\mathbb{C}_{p}
being the
p
-adic competion
with respect to the
p
adic norm induced by
\sigma_{p}
.
Then
\hat{G}_{\sigma_{p}}:=Gal(\mathbb{C}_{p}/\hat{k}_{\sigma_{p}})\subset G:=Gal(\bar{k}/k)
.

(i)

Let
\alpha\in H_{et}^{j}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
.
Consider then its associated De Rham class (see definition
2
)

\displaystyle w(\alpha):=H^{j}R\alpha(X)(\alpha\otimes 1)\in H^{j}_{DR}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}\mathbb{B}_{dr,\mathbb{C}_{p}}.

Then
\alpha\in H_{et}^{j}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})(l)^{\hat{G}_{\sigma_{p}}}
if and only if

w(\alpha)\in F^{l}H^{j}_{DR}(X_{\hat{k}_{\sigma_{p}}})=F^{l}H^{j}_{DR}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}})\cap H^{j}_{DR}(X_{\hat{k}_{\sigma_{p}}})
.
That is we have

H_{et}^{j}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})(l)^{\hat{G}_{\sigma_{p}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}=<F^{l}H^{j}_{DR}(X_{\hat{k}_{\sigma_{p}}})\cap H_{et}^{j}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})>_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\subset H^{j}_{et}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}},\mathbb{Z}_{p,X^{et}})\otimes\mathbb{B}_{dr,\mathbb{C}_{p}},

where
<->_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
denote the
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
vector space generated by
(-)
.
Note that
H_{et}^{j}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
and
F^{l}H^{j}_{DR}(X_{\hat{k}_{\sigma_{p}}})

are canonically embedded as subabelian groups of

H^{j}_{et}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}},\mathbb{Z}_{p,X^{et}})\otimes\mathbb{B}_{dr,\mathbb{C}_{p}}

by
(-)\otimes 1
and
\alpha(X)\circ((-)\otimes 1)
respectively.

(ii)

Let
\alpha\in H_{et}^{j}(X_{\bar{k}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})(l)
. Consider, see (i),

w(\alpha):=w(\pi_{\bar{k}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}(X)^{*}\alpha)\in H^{j}_{DR}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}\mathbb{B}_{dr,\mathbb{C}_{p}}.

Then
\alpha\in H_{et}^{j}(X_{\bar{k}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})(l)^{G}
if and only if

w(\alpha)\in F^{l}H^{j}_{DR}(X)=F^{l}H^{j}_{DR}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}})\cap H^{j}_{DR}(X)
.
That is we have

H_{et}^{j}(X_{\bar{k}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})(l)^{G}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}=<F^{l}H^{j}_{DR}(X)\cap H_{et}^{j}(X_{\bar{k}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})>_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\subset H^{j}_{et}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}},\mathbb{Z}_{p,X^{et}})\otimes\mathbb{B}_{dr,\mathbb{C}_{p}},

where
<->_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
denote the
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
vector space generated by
(-)
.
Note that
H_{et}^{j}(X_{\bar{k}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
and
F^{l}H^{j}_{DR}(X)
are canonically embedded as subabelian groups of

H^{j}_{et}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}},\mathbb{Z}_{p,X^{et}})\otimes\mathbb{B}_{dr,\mathbb{C}_{p}}

by
(-)\otimes 1
and
\alpha(X)\circ((-)\otimes 1)
respectively.

Proof.

(i):Follows immediately from the fact that
H^{j}R\alpha(X)

is a filtered quasi-isomorphism compatible with the Galois action of
\hat{G}_{\sigma_{p}}
by
[
16
]
.

(ii):Let
\alpha\in H^{j}_{et}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{G}

Take a basis
((\alpha_{i})_{1\leq i\leq t},(\alpha_{i})_{t+1\leq i\leq s})\in H^{j}_{et}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
such
that for
1\leq i\leq t
,
\alpha_{i}\in H^{j}_{et}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{G}
. We have

w(\alpha)=\sum_{1\leq i\leq s}\lambda_{i,w(\alpha)}w(\alpha_{i})\in H^{j}_{DR}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}})

Assume by absurd that
w(\alpha)\notin H^{j}_{DR}(X)
.
There exist a finite type extenion
k^{\prime}/k
,
k^{\prime}\subset\mathbb{C}_{p}
(depending on
w(\alpha)
)
such that
w(\alpha)\in H^{j}_{DR}(X_{k^{\prime}})
.
By assumption the orbit
Aut(k^{\prime}/k)\cdot w(\alpha)\subset H_{DR}(X_{k^{\prime}})
of
w(\alpha)
under
Aut(k^{\prime}/k)

is non trivial (i.e. contain more then one element).
Then there exist a prime number
l
and an embedding
\sigma^{\prime}_{l}:k^{\prime}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}_{l}

such that the extension
\hat{k}^{\prime}_{\sigma^{\prime}_{l}}/\hat{k}_{\sigma_{l}}
is non trivial
(i.e.
\hat{k}^{\prime}_{\sigma^{\prime}_{l}}\neq\hat{k}_{\sigma_{l}}
) where
\sigma_{l}=\sigma^{\prime}_{l|k}

and such that
w(\alpha):=\pi_{k^{\prime}/\hat{k}^{\prime}_{\sigma^{\prime}_{l}}}(X_{k^{\prime}})^{*}w(\alpha)\notin H^{j}_{DR}(X_{\hat{k}_{\sigma_{l}}})
,
where
\pi_{k^{\prime}/\hat{k}^{\prime}_{\sigma^{\prime}_{l}}}(X_{k^{\prime}}):X_{\hat{k}^{\prime}_{\sigma^{\prime}_{l}}}\to X_{k^{\prime}}
is the projection,
recall that
H^{j}_{DR}(X_{k^{\prime}})=H^{j}_{DR}(X)\otimes_{k}k^{\prime}
.
By injectivity of

\pi_{k^{\prime}/\hat{k}^{\prime}_{\sigma^{\prime}_{l}}}(X_{k^{\prime}})^{*}:H^{j}_{DR}(X_{k^{\prime}})\to H^{j}_{DR}(X_{\hat{k}^{\prime}_{\sigma^{\prime}_{l}}}),

the orbit
Gal(\hat{k}^{\prime}_{\sigma^{\prime}_{l}}/\hat{k}_{\sigma_{l}})\cdot\lambda w(\alpha)\subset H_{DR}(X_{\hat{k}^{\prime}_{\sigma^{\prime}_{l}}})

of
w(\alpha):=\pi_{k^{\prime}/\hat{k}^{\prime}_{\sigma^{\prime}_{l}}}(X_{k^{\prime}})^{*}w(\alpha)

under
Gal(\hat{k}^{\prime}_{\sigma^{\prime}_{l}}/\hat{k}_{\sigma^{\prime}_{l}})\subset Aut(k^{\prime}/k)
is non trivial (i.e. contain more then one element).
By the main result of
[
16
]
(see above), we get

H^{j}R\alpha(X_{\mathbb{C}_{l}})^{-1}(\lambda w(\alpha))\notin(H^{j}_{et}(X_{\bar{k}^{\prime}},\mathbb{Z}_{l})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{l}}\mathbb{B}_{dr,\mathbb{C}_{l}})^{\hat{G}_{\sigma^{\prime}_{l}}}.

Consider the pairing of
G
modules

\delta(-,-):H^{k}_{pet}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}},\mathbb{B}_{dr,\mathbb{C}_{p}})\otimes_{k^{\prime}}H^{l}_{pet}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{l}},\mathbb{B}_{dr,\mathbb{C}_{l}})\to H^{k+l}_{pet}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}\otimes_{k^{\prime}}\mathbb{C}_{l}},\mathbb{B}_{dr,\mathbb{C}_{p}}\otimes_{k^{\prime}}\mathbb{B}_{dr,\mathbb{C}_{l}})

and

\displaystyle\alpha(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}\otimes_{k^{\prime}}\mathbb{C}_{l}}):\pi_{p}^{*}\mathbb{B}_{dr,X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}}\otimes_{k^{\prime}}\pi_{l}^{*}\mathbb{B}_{dr,X_{\mathbb{C}_{l}}}\xrightarrow{\alpha(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}})\otimes\alpha(X_{\mathbb{C}_{l}})}

\displaystyle\pi_{p}^{*}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}}\otimes_{O_{X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}}}O\mathbb{B}_{dr,X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}})\otimes_{k^{\prime}}\pi_{l}^{*}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{\mathbb{C}_{l}}}\otimes_{O_{X_{\mathbb{C}_{l}}}}O\mathbb{B}_{dr,X_{\mathbb{C}_{l}}})\xrightarrow{w(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}\otimes_{k^{\prime}}\mathbb{C}_{l}}}\otimes_{\pi_{p}^{*}O_{X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}}\otimes_{k^{\prime}}\pi_{l}^{*}O_{X_{\mathbb{C}_{l}}}}O\mathbb{B}_{dr,X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}}\otimes_{k^{\prime}}O\mathbb{B}_{dr,X_{\mathbb{C}_{l}}}=:DR(X)(O\mathbb{B}_{dr,X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}}\otimes_{k^{\prime}}O\mathbb{B}_{dr,X_{\mathbb{C}_{l}}})

is the canonical map in

C_{\hat{G}_{\sigma^{\prime}_{p}}\times\hat{G}_{\sigma^{\prime}_{l}}}(X^{pet}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}\otimes_{k^{\prime}}\mathbb{C}_{l}})
and

•

\pi_{p}:=\pi_{\mathbb{C}_{p}/\mathbb{C}_{p}\otimes_{k^{\prime}}\mathbb{C}_{l}}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}):X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}\otimes_{k^{\prime}}\mathbb{C}_{l}}\to X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}

•

\pi_{l}:=\pi_{\mathbb{C}_{l}/\mathbb{C}_{p}\otimes_{k^{\prime}}\mathbb{C}_{l}}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{l}}):X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}\otimes_{k^{\prime}}\mathbb{C}_{l}}\to X_{\mathbb{C}_{l}}

are the base change maps. By the commutative diagram of
\hat{G}_{\sigma^{\prime}_{p}}\times\hat{G}_{\sigma^{\prime}_{l}}
modules

1)\circ\pi_{\hat{k}^{\prime}_{\sigma_{l}}/\mathbb{C}_{l}}(X_{k^{\prime}})^{*}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 240.8957pt\raise-42.51082pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise we have for
\beta_{p}\in H^{j}_{pet}(X_{\bar{k}^{\prime}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{B}_{dr,\mathbb{C}_{p}}

\beta_{l}\in H^{j}_{pet}(X_{\bar{k}^{\prime}},\mathbb{Z}_{l})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{l}}\mathbb{B}_{dr,\mathbb{C}_{l}}
,

\alpha(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}\otimes_{k^{\prime}}\mathbb{C}_{l}})(\delta(\beta_{p},\beta_{l}))=\alpha(X_{\mathbb{C}_{l}})(\beta_{p})\cdot\alpha(X_{\mathbb{C}_{l}})(\beta_{l})\in\mathbb{H}^{j}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}\otimes_{k^{\prime}}\mathbb{C}_{l}},DR(X)(O\mathbb{B}_{dr,\mathbb{C}_{p}}\otimes_{k^{\prime}}O\mathbb{B}_{dr,\mathbb{C}_{l}})).

Note that since

\pi_{\bar{k}^{\prime}/(\mathbb{C}_{p}\otimes_{k^{\prime}}\mathbb{C}_{l})}(X_{\bar{k}^{\prime}}):X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}\otimes_{k^{\prime}}\mathbb{C}_{l}}\to X_{\bar{k}^{\prime}}

is flat
((-)\otimes 1)\circ\pi_{\bar{k}^{\prime}/(\mathbb{C}_{p}\otimes_{k^{\prime}}\mathbb{C}_{l})}(X_{\bar{k}^{\prime}})^{*}
and

((-)\otimes 1)\circ\pi_{\bar{k}^{\prime}/(\mathbb{C}_{p}\otimes_{k^{\prime}}\mathbb{C}_{l})}(X_{\bar{k}^{\prime}})^{*}
are injective,
(the morphism involved in the base change are without torsion).
Denote
d=\mathop{\rm dim}\nolimits(X)
. Consider the canonical projection

\displaystyle\pi:X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}\times X_{\mathbb{C}_{l}}\to X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}\otimes_{k^{\prime}}\mathbb{C}_{l}},\;\mbox{given by on}\;X^{o}\subset X\;\mbox{open affine}

\displaystyle\pi((x_{1},\cdots,x_{d}),(x^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,x^{\prime}_{d})):=(x_{1}\otimes x^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,x_{d}\otimes x^{\prime}_{d}),

where
X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}\times X_{\mathbb{C}_{l}}
is endowed with the product topology.
Then the commutative diagram

\textstyle{H^{j}_{pet}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}\otimes_{k^{\prime}}\mathbb{C}_{l}},\mathbb{B}_{dr,\mathbb{C}_{p}}\otimes_{k^{\prime}}\mathbb{B}_{dr,\mathbb{C}_{l}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\alpha(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}\otimes_{k^{\prime}}\mathbb{C}_{l}})}
\textstyle{H^{j}_{pet}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}\times X_{\mathbb{C}_{l}},\pi^{*}(\mathbb{B}_{dr,\mathbb{C}_{p}}\otimes_{k^{\prime}}\mathbb{B}_{dr,\mathbb{C}_{l}}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{w(-)\circ(\pi^{*}\alpha(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}})\otimes\pi^{*}\alpha(X_{\mathbb{C}_{l}}))}
\textstyle{\mathbb{H}_{pet}^{j}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}\otimes_{k^{\prime}}\mathbb{C}_{l}},DR(X)(O\mathbb{B}_{dr,X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}}\otimes_{k^{\prime}}O\mathbb{B}_{dr,X_{\mathbb{C}_{l}}}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{*}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{H}_{pet}^{j}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}\times X_{\mathbb{C}_{l}},DR(X)(O\mathbb{B}_{dr,X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}}\otimes_{k^{\prime}}O\mathbb{B}_{dr,X_{\mathbb{C}_{l}}}))}

together with the
p
adic Poincare lemma on
X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}
and the
l
adic Poincare lemma on
X_{\mathbb{C}_{l}}
,
the fact that
\pi^{*}
is injective
(note that the product topology is less fine then the pro-etale topology on
X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}\times_{k^{\prime}}X_{\mathbb{C}_{l}}

and that the map

O_{X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}\otimes_{k^{\prime}}\mathbb{C}_{l}}}\to O_{X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}\times_{k^{\prime}}X_{\mathbb{C}_{l}}}
is torsion free),
show that
\alpha(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}\otimes_{k^{\prime}}\mathbb{C}_{l}})
is injective. Hence

\alpha\otimes 1=\delta(\alpha,1)=\delta(1,H^{j}R\alpha(X_{\mathbb{C}_{l}})^{-1}(w(\alpha)))\in H^{j}_{pet}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}\otimes_{k^{\prime}}\mathbb{C}_{l}},\mathbb{B}_{dr,\mathbb{C}_{p}}\otimes_{k^{\prime}}\mathbb{B}_{dr,\mathbb{C}_{l}}),

that is there exists
\lambda_{\alpha}\in k^{\prime}
such that

\alpha=\lambda_{\alpha}H^{j}R\alpha(X_{\mathbb{C}_{l}})^{-1}(w(\alpha))=H^{j}R\alpha(X_{\mathbb{C}_{l}})^{-1}(\lambda_{\alpha}w(\alpha))\in H^{j}_{pet}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}\otimes_{k^{\prime}}\mathbb{C}_{l}},\mathbb{B}_{dr,\mathbb{C}_{p}}\otimes_{k^{\prime}}\mathbb{B}_{dr,\mathbb{C}_{l}}).

Since
\alpha
and
w(\alpha)
are
\hat{G}_{\sigma^{\prime}_{p}}
invariant,
\lambda_{\alpha}\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.
This gives

\alpha=H^{j}R\alpha(X_{\mathbb{C}_{l}})^{-1}(\lambda_{\alpha}w(\alpha))\notin H^{j}_{pet}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}\otimes_{k^{\prime}}\mathbb{C}_{l}},\mathbb{Z}_{p}\otimes\mathbb{B}_{dr,\mathbb{C}_{l}})^{\hat{G}_{\sigma^{\prime}_{l}}}.

Contradiction. We thus have
w(\alpha)\in H^{j}_{DR}(X)
.
Conversely if
\alpha\notin H_{et}^{j}(X_{\bar{k}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})(l)^{G}
, we get similarly
w(\alpha)\notin H^{j}_{DR}(X)
.
The result then follows from (i) and the equality

F^{l}H^{j}_{DR}(X)=F^{l}H^{j}_{DR}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}})\cap H^{j}_{DR}(X)

given by the filtered isomorphism in
C_{fil}(\bar{X}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}})

w(k/\mathbb{C}_{p}):(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\bar{X}}(\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits D),F_{b})\otimes_{k}\mathbb{C}_{p}\xrightarrow{\sim}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\bar{X}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}}(\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits D_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}),F_{b})

which say that the Hodge filtration is defined over
k
: see section 2.
∎

Theorem 1
.

(i)

Let
k
a field of finite type over
\mathbb{Q}
.
Denote
\bar{k}
its algebraic closure and
G=Gal(\bar{k}/k)
its absolute Galois group.
Let
X\in\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(k)
. Let
\sigma:k\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
an embedding.
Let
p\in\mathbb{N}\backslash\delta(k,X)
a prime number. Let
\sigma_{p}:k\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}_{p}
an embedding.
For each
j,l\in\mathbb{Z}
, we get from proposition
5
(i), proposition
4
,
and proposition
1
, a canonical injective map

H^{j}\iota_{p,ev}^{G,l}(X):H_{et}^{j}(X_{\bar{k}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})(l)^{G}\hookrightarrow F^{l}H^{j}(X^{an}_{\mathbb{C}},2i\pi\mathbb{Q})\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{Q}_{p},\;\alpha\mapsto H^{j}\iota_{p,ev}^{G,d}(X)(\alpha):=ev(X)(w(\alpha)),

with
w(\alpha):=H^{j}R\alpha(X)(\alpha\otimes 1)\in H^{j}_{DR}(X)\subset H^{j}_{DR}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}\mathbb{B}_{st,\mathbb{C}_{p}}
(see definition
2
), and

F^{l}H^{j}(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an},2i\pi\mathbb{Q}):=H^{j}_{\mathop{\rm sing}\nolimits}(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an},2i\pi\mathbb{Q})\cap F^{l}H^{j}_{DR}(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an})\subset H^{j}(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an},\mathbb{C}).

By construction, for
f:X^{\prime}\to X
a morphism with
X,X^{\prime}\in\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(k)
and
p\in\mathbb{N}\backslash\delta(k,X,X^{\prime})
a prime number,
we have the commutative diagram

(ii)

Let
K\subset\mathbb{C}_{p}
a
p
-adic field such that
Frac(W(O_{K}))=K
.
Let
X\in\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(K)
such that its canonical model

X^{\mathcal{O}}\in\mathop{\rm Sch}\nolimits/O_{K}
has good or semi-stable reduction modulo
p
.
Denote
G_{p}=Gal(\bar{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}/K)
its absolute Galois group.
Let
\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
an embedding.
For each
j,l\in\mathbb{Z}
, we get from proposition
5
(i), proposition
4
,
and proposition
1
, a canonical injective map

H^{j}\iota_{ev}^{G_{p},l}(X):H_{et}^{j}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})(l)^{G_{p}}\hookrightarrow F^{l}H^{j}(X^{an}_{\mathbb{C}},2i\pi\mathbb{Q})\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{Q}_{p},\;\alpha\mapsto H^{j}\iota_{ev}^{G_{p},l}(X)(\alpha):=ev(X)(w(\alpha)),

with
w(\alpha):=H^{j}R\alpha(X)(\alpha\otimes 1)\in H^{j}_{DR}(X)\subset H^{j}_{DR}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}\mathbb{B}_{st,\mathbb{C}_{p}}
(see definition
2
), and

F^{l}H^{j}(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an},2i\pi\mathbb{Q}):=H^{j}_{\mathop{\rm sing}\nolimits}(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an},2i\pi\mathbb{Q})\cap F^{l}H^{j}_{DR}(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an})\subset H^{j}(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an},\mathbb{C}).

By construction, for
f:X^{\prime}\to X
a morphism with
X,X^{\prime}\in\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(K)
,we have the commutative diagram

Note that (ii) implies (i).

Proof.

(i):Follows from (ii).

(ii):By proposition
5
(i),

w(\alpha)\in F^{l}H^{j}_{DR}(X_{\hat{k}_{\sigma_{p}}})\subset H^{j}_{DR}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}\mathbb{B}_{st,\mathbb{C}_{p}}
.
By proposition
4
,

\displaystyle w(\alpha)=\sum_{1\leq i\leq r}\lambda_{i}w_{Li}\in H^{j}_{DR}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}),\;(w_{Li})_{1\leq i\leq r}\in\mathbb{H}_{pet}^{j}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}},\Omega^{\bullet\geq l}_{X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}},\mathop{\rm log}\nolimits,\mathcal{O}}),\;(\lambda_{i})_{1\leq i\leq r}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}.

Then, for each
1\leq i\leq r
,

•

by proposition
1
,
ev(X)(w_{Li})\in H^{j}_{\mathop{\rm sing}\nolimits}(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an},2i\pi\mathbb{Q})
,

•

w_{Li}:=H^{j}OL_{X}(w_{Li})\in F^{l}H^{j}_{DR}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}})
,

that is,
w_{Li}\in F^{l}H^{j}(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an},2i\pi\mathbb{Q})
. Hence,

ev(X)(w(\alpha))=\sum_{1\leq i\leq r}\lambda_{i}ev(X)(w_{Li})\in F^{l}H^{j}(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an},2i\pi\mathbb{Q})\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}.

∎

Remark 3
.

Let
k
a field of finite type over
\mathbb{Q}
. Let
X\in\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(k)
.
Let
p\in\mathbb{N}
be a prime number.
Let
\sigma_{p}:k\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}_{p}
and
\sigma:k\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
be embeddings.
Note that for
w\in\mathbb{H}^{j}(X,\Omega_{X^{et}}^{\bullet})
such that

ev(X)(w)\in H^{j}_{\mathop{\rm sing}\nolimits}(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an},2i\pi\mathbb{Q})
,

w
is NOT logarithmic in general and
T(X)(ev(X)(w))\in H^{j}_{et}(X_{\bar{k}},\mathbb{Q}_{p})

is NOT
G=Gal(\bar{k}/k)
equivariant in general, where

T(X):H^{j}_{sing}(X^{an}_{\mathbb{C}},\mathbb{Q})\xrightarrow{/p^{*}}H^{j}_{sing}(X^{an}_{\mathbb{C}},\mathbb{Q}_{p})\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{j}_{et}(X^{an}_{\mathbb{C}},\mathbb{Q}_{p})=H^{j}_{et}(X_{\bar{k}},\mathbb{Q}_{p}).

is given in section 2.

4
The complex and etale Abel Jacobi maps and normal function

4.1
The complex Abel Jacobi map for higher Chow group and complex normal functions

Let
k
a field of finite type over
\mathbb{Q}
.
Consider an embedding
\sigma:k\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
.
Then
k\subset\bar{k}\subset\mathbb{C}
, where
\bar{k}
is the algebraic closure of
k
.
We have then the quasi-isomorphism

\alpha(X):\mathbb{C}_{X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an}}\hookrightarrow\Omega^{\bullet}_{X^{an}_{\mathbb{C}}}

in
C(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an})
.

•

For
X\in\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(k)
, we consider

((H^{j}_{DR}(X),F),H^{j}(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an},\mathbb{Z}),H^{j}\alpha(X))\in MHM_{k,gm}(k)\subset\mathop{\rm Vect}\nolimits_{fil}(k)\times_{I}\mathop{\rm Ab}\nolimits

where
F
is the Hodge filtration on
H^{j}_{DR}(X)\otimes_{k}\mathbb{C}
and

H^{j}R\Gamma(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an},\alpha(X)):H^{j}(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an},\mathbb{C})\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{j}_{DR}(X)\otimes_{k}\mathbb{C}

Recall the geometric mixed Hodge structures (see
[
5
]
) are mixed Hodge structure by the
Hodge decomposition theorem on smooth proper complex varieties.

•

For
f:X\to S
a morphism with
S,X\in\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(k)
, we consider

(H^{j}Rf_{*Hdg}(O_{X},F_{b}),H^{j}Rf_{*}\mathbb{Z}_{X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an}},H^{j}Rf_{*}\alpha(X))\in MHM_{k,gm}(S)\subset\mathop{\rm PSh}\nolimits_{\mathcal{D}(1,0)fil}(S)\times_{I}P_{fil,k}(S_{\mathbb{C}}^{an})

where

H^{j}Rf_{*}\alpha(X):H^{j}Rf_{*}\mathbb{C}_{X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an}}\xrightarrow{\sim}DR(S)(o_{fil}H^{j}Rf_{*Hdg}(O_{X},F_{b})\otimes_{k}\mathbb{C}=H^{j}\int_{f}O_{X}\otimes_{k}\mathbb{C})

Recall the geometric mixed Hodge modules (see
[
5
]
) are mixed Hodge modules by
a theorem of Saito for proper morphisms of smooth complex varieties.

Let
X\in\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(k)
. We have for
j,d\in\mathbb{N}
, the generalized Jacobian

\displaystyle J_{\sigma}^{j,d}(X):=H^{j}(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an},\mathbb{C})/(F^{d}H^{j}(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an},\mathbb{C})\oplus H^{j}(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an},\mathbb{Z}))

where
F
is given the Hodge filtration on
H^{j}_{DR}(X)\otimes_{k}\mathbb{C}
and

H^{j}R\Gamma(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an},\alpha(X)):H^{j}(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an},\mathbb{C})\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{j}_{DR}(X)\otimes_{k}\mathbb{C}.

If
X\in\mathop{\rm PSmVar}\nolimits(k)
and
2d\geq n
,
J^{j,d}(X)
is a complex torus since

((H^{j}_{DR}(X),F),H^{j}(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an},\mathbb{Z}),H^{j}R\Gamma(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an},\alpha(X)))\in HM_{k,gm}(k)\subset MHM_{k,gm}(k)

is a pure Hodge structure.
For
X\in\mathop{\rm PSmVar}\nolimits(k)
, we have a canonical isomorphism of abelian groups

\displaystyle I_{\sigma}^{j,d}(X):J_{\sigma}^{j,d}(X)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathop{\rm Ext}\nolimits^{1}_{MHM_{k,gm}(k)}(\mathbb{Z}_{\mathop{\rm pt}\nolimits}^{Hdg}(d),(H^{j}_{DR}(X),H^{j}(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an},\mathbb{Z}),H^{j}R\Gamma(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an},\alpha(X)))).

Definition 3
.

Let
X\in\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(k)
irreducible.
Let
\bar{X}\in\mathop{\rm PSmVar}\nolimits(k)
a compactification of
X
with
D:=\bar{X}\backslash X\subset\bar{X}
a normal crossing divisor.
The map of complexes of abelian groups (see
[
4
]
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{X}:\mathcal{Z}^{d}(X,\bullet)\to C_{\bullet}^{\mathcal{D}}(\bar{X}_{\mathbb{C}}^{an},D_{\mathbb{C}}^{an}),Z\mapsto\mathcal{R}_{X}:=(T_{Z},\Omega_{Z},R_{Z})

where
C_{\bullet}^{\mathcal{D}}(\bar{X}_{\mathbb{C}}^{an},D_{\mathbb{C}}^{an})
is the Deligne homology complex
induces the complex Abel Jacobi map for higher Chow groups

\displaystyle AJ_{\sigma}(X):\mathcal{Z}^{d}(X,n)^{\partial=0}_{hom}\to J_{\sigma}^{2d-1-n,d}(X),Z\mapsto AJ_{\sigma}(X)(Z):=D^{-1}(R^{\prime}_{Z}),

\displaystyle R^{\prime}_{Z}=R_{Z}-\Omega^{\prime}_{Z}+T^{\prime}_{Z},\,;\mbox{with}\;\partial T^{\prime}_{Z}=T_{Z},\,\partial\Omega^{\prime}_{Z}=\Omega_{Z}

where

D:C_{\bullet}^{\mathcal{D}}(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an})\to C_{\bullet}^{\mathcal{D}}(\bar{X}_{\mathbb{C}}^{an},D_{\mathbb{C}}^{an})

is the Poincare dual for Deligne homology.

Theorem 2
.

Let
k\subset\mathbb{C}
a subfield. Let
X\in\mathop{\rm PSmVar}\nolimits(k)
.

(i)

For
Z\in\mathcal{Z}^{d}(X,n)^{\partial=0}_{hom}
, we have

\displaystyle AJ_{\sigma}(X)(Z)=I_{\sigma}^{j,d}(X)^{-1}(0\to(H^{2d-1-n}_{DR}(X),H^{2d-1-n}_{\mathop{\rm sing}\nolimits}(X^{an}_{\mathbb{C}},\mathbb{Z}),H^{2d-1-n}R\Gamma(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an},\alpha(X)))

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{(j^{*},j^{*},0)}

\displaystyle(H^{2d-1}_{DR}((X\times\square^{n})\backslash|Z|),H^{2d-1}_{\mathop{\rm sing}\nolimits}(((X\times\square^{n})\backslash|Z|)^{an}_{\mathbb{C}},\mathbb{Z}),H^{2d-1-n}R\Gamma((X\times\square^{n})\backslash|Z|)_{\mathbb{C}}^{an},\alpha(X\times\square^{n}))^{[Z]}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{(\partial,\partial,0)}

\displaystyle(H_{DR,|Z|}^{2d}(X\times\square^{n}),H_{\mathop{\rm sing}\nolimits,|Z|}^{2d}((X\times\square^{n})^{an}_{\mathbb{C}},\mathbb{Z}),R\Gamma_{|Z|}((X\times\square^{n})_{\mathbb{C}}^{an},\alpha(X\times\square^{n})))^{[Z]}=\mathbb{Z}_{\mathop{\rm pt}\nolimits}^{Hdg}(n-d)\to 0)

where
j:(X\times\square^{n})\backslash|Z|\hookrightarrow X\times\square^{n}
is the open embedding and

H^{2d}_{Hdg,|Z|}(X\times\square^{n})^{[Z]}\subset H^{2d}_{Hdg,|Z|}(X\times\square^{n}),\;H^{2d-1}_{Hdg}((X\times\square^{n})\backslash|Z|)^{[Z]}\subset H^{2d-1}_{Hdg}((X\times\square^{n})\backslash|Z|).

are the subobjects given by the pullback of the class of
Z
(see section 2).

(ii)

Let
Z\in\mathcal{Z}^{d}(X,n)^{\partial=0}_{hom}
. Then
AJ_{\sigma}(X)(Z)=0
if and only if there exist

w\in H^{2d-1}_{DR}((X\times\square^{n})\backslash|Z|)^{[Z]}
such that

–

w\in F^{d}H^{2d-1}_{DR}((X\times\square^{n})\backslash|Z|)
,

–

ev((X\times\square^{n})\backslash|Z|)(w)\in H^{2d-1}_{\mathop{\rm sing}\nolimits}(((X\times\square^{n})\backslash|Z|)^{an}_{\mathbb{C}},\mathbb{Z}(2i\pi))

–

\partial w\neq 0
.

Proof.

(i):See
[
6
]
.

(ii):Follows from (i)
∎

Let
f:X\to S
a morphism with
S,X\in\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(k)
.
We have for
j,d\in\mathbb{N}
such that
H^{j}Rf_{*}\mathbb{C}_{X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an}}
is a local system
and
F^{d}H^{j}Rf_{*Hdg}(O_{X},F_{b})\subset H^{j}\int_{f}(O_{X})
are locally free sub
O_{S}
modules,
the generalized relative intermediate Jacobian

\displaystyle J_{\sigma}^{j,d}(X/S):=(H^{j}Rf_{*}\mathbb{C}_{X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an}}\otimes O_{S_{\mathbb{C}}^{an}})/(F^{d}(H^{j}Rf_{*}\mathbb{C}_{X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an}}\otimes O_{S_{\mathbb{C}}^{an}})\oplus H^{j}Rf_{*}\mathbb{Z}_{X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an}})

where
F
is given by the Hodge filtration on
H^{j}\int_{f}(O_{X})
and

H^{j}Rf_{*}\alpha(X):H^{j}Rf_{*}\mathbb{C}_{X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an}}\xrightarrow{\sim}DR(S)(H^{j}\int_{f}(O_{X})).

A generalized normal function is then a section
\nu\in\Gamma(S_{\mathbb{C}}^{an},J_{\sigma}^{j,d}(X/S))

which is horizontal (i.e.
\nabla\nu=0
).
For
s\in S
, we get immediately that
i_{s}^{*mod}J_{\sigma}^{j,d}(X/S)=J_{\sigma}^{j,d}(X_{s})
.
In particular we get for
f:X\to S
a smooth projective morphism with
S,X\in\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(k)

and
j,d\in\mathbb{N}
, the relative intermediate Jacobian

\displaystyle J_{\sigma}^{j,d}(X/S):=(H^{j}Rf_{*}\mathbb{C}_{X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an}}\otimes O_{S_{\mathbb{C}}^{an}})/(F^{d}(H^{j}Rf_{*}\mathbb{C}_{X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an}}\otimes O_{S_{\mathbb{C}}^{an}})\oplus H^{j}Rf_{*}\mathbb{Z}_{X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an}})

where
F
is given by the Hodge filtration on
H^{j}\int_{f}(O_{X})=H^{j}Rf_{*}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X/S}
and
H^{j}Rf_{*}\alpha(X)
.
A normal function is then a section
\nu\in\Gamma(S_{\mathbb{C}}^{an},J_{\sigma}^{j,d}(X/S))
which is horizontal.
For
f:X\to S
a smooth projective morphism with
S,X\in\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(k)
, we have a canonical isomorphism of abelian groups

\displaystyle I_{\sigma}^{j,d}(X/S):J_{\sigma}^{j,d}(X/S)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathop{\rm Ext}\nolimits^{1}_{MHM(S)}(\mathbb{Z}_{S}^{Hdg}(d),(H^{j}Rf_{*Hdg}(O_{X},F_{b}),H^{j}Rf_{*}\mathbb{Z}_{X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an}},H^{j}Rf_{*}\alpha(X))).

Definition-Proposition 1
.

Let
f:X\to S
a morphism with
S,X\in\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(k)
. Let
j:S^{o}\hookrightarrow S
an open subset such that
for all
j,d\in\mathbb{Z}
,
j^{*}H^{j}Rf_{*}\mathbb{C}_{X_{\mathbb{C}}^{an}}
is a local system
and
j^{*}F^{d}H^{j}Rf_{*Hdg}(O_{X},F_{b})\subset j^{*}H^{j}\int_{f}(O_{X})
is a locally free sub
O_{S}
module.
Let
\sigma:k\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
an embedding.
Let
d,n\in\mathbb{N}
. We have then, denoting
X^{o}:=X\times_{S}S^{o}
and using definition
3
, the map

\displaystyle AJ_{\sigma}(X^{o}/S^{o}):\mathcal{Z}^{d}(X,n)^{f,\partial=0}_{fhom}\to\Gamma(S_{\mathbb{C}}^{o,an},J_{\sigma}^{2d-n-1,d}(X^{o}/S^{o}))\subset\Gamma(S_{\mathbb{C}}^{o,an},\oplus_{s\in S_{\mathbb{C}}^{o,an}}i_{s*}J_{\sigma^{\prime}}^{2d-n-1,d}(X_{s})),

\displaystyle Z\mapsto AJ_{\sigma}(X^{o}/S^{o})(Z):=\nu_{Z}:=((s\in S_{\mathbb{C}}^{o})\mapsto(AJ_{\sigma^{\prime}}(X_{s})(Z_{s})\in J_{\sigma^{\prime}}^{2d-n-1,d}(X_{s})))

where
\mathcal{Z}^{d}(X,n)^{f,\partial=0}_{fhom}\subset\mathcal{Z}^{d}(X,n)^{f,\partial=0}
denote the sub-abelian group
consisting of algebraic cycles
Z
with
Z_{s}:=i_{s}^{*}Z\in\mathcal{Z}^{d}(X_{s},n)^{\partial=0}_{hom}
,
and
\sigma^{\prime}:k(s)\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
is the embedding given by
s
extending
\sigma:k\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
,
denoting again
s:=\pi{k/\mathbb{C}}(S)(s)\in S
,
\pi_{k/\mathbb{C}}(S):S_{\mathbb{C}}\to S
being the projection.

Proof.

Standard : to show that

\nu_{Z}:=(s\in S_{\mathbb{C}}^{o})\mapsto AJ_{\sigma}(X_{s})(Z_{s})\in\Gamma(S_{\mathbb{C}}^{o,an},\oplus_{s\in S_{\mathbb{C}}^{o,an}}i_{s*}J_{\sigma}^{2d-n-1,d}(X_{s}))

is holomorphic and horizontal
we consider a compactification
\bar{f}:\bar{X}\to S
of
f
with
\bar{X}\in\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(k)
and
use trivializations of

f:(\bar{X}_{\mathbb{C}}^{o,an},(\bar{X}\backslash X)_{\mathbb{C}}^{o,an})\to S_{\mathbb{C}}^{o,an}

which gives trivialization of the local system
j^{*}Rf_{*}\mathbb{Z}_{S_{\mathbb{C}}^{an}}
(see
[
4
]
for example).
∎

Corollary 1
.

Let
f:X\to S
a smooth projective morphism with
S,X\in\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(k)
.
Let
d,n\in\mathbb{N}
. For
Z\in\mathcal{Z}^{d}(X,n)^{f,\partial=0}_{fhom}
, we have

\displaystyle AJ_{\sigma}(X/S)(Z)=I_{\sigma}^{j,d}(X/S)^{-1}(0\to(H^{2d-1-n}Rf_{*Hdg}(O_{X},F_{b}),H^{2d-1-n}Rf_{*}\mathbb{Z}_{X^{an}_{\mathbb{C}}},H^{2d-1-n}Rf_{*}\alpha(X))

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{(j^{*},j^{*},0)}(H^{2d-1}R(f\circ j)_{*Hdg}(O_{(X\times\square^{n})\backslash|Z|},F_{b}),H^{2d-1}R(f\circ j)_{*}\mathbb{Z}_{((X\times\square^{n})\backslash|Z|)^{an}_{\mathbb{C}}},

\displaystyle H^{2d-1}R(f\circ j)_{*}\alpha((X\times\square^{n})\backslash|Z|))^{[Z]}\xrightarrow{(\partial,\partial,0)}

\displaystyle(H^{2d}Rf_{*Hdg}R\Gamma^{Hdg}_{|Z|}(O_{X\times\square^{n}},F_{b}),(H^{2d}Rf_{*}R\Gamma_{|Z|}\mathbb{Z}_{(X\times\square^{n})^{an}_{\mathbb{C}}}),Rf_{*}\Gamma_{|Z|}\alpha(X\times\square^{n}))^{[Z]}=\mathbb{Z}_{S}^{Hdg}(n-d)\to 0)

where
j:(X\times\square^{n})\backslash|Z|\hookrightarrow X\times\square^{n}
is the open embedding and

\displaystyle(H^{2d}Rf_{*Hdg}R\Gamma^{Hdg}_{|Z|}\mathbb{Z}_{X\times\square^{n}}^{Hdg})^{[Z]}\subset H^{2d}Rf_{*Hdg}R\Gamma^{Hdg}_{|Z|}\mathbb{Z}_{X\times\square^{n}}^{Hdg},

\displaystyle(H^{2d}R(f\circ j)_{*Hdg}\mathbb{Z}_{(X\times\square^{n})\backslash|Z|}^{Hdg})^{[Z]}\subset H^{2d}R(f\circ j)_{*Hdg}\mathbb{Z}_{(X\times\square^{n})\backslash|Z|}^{Hdg}.

are the subobjects given by the pullback of the class of
Z
.

Proof.

Follows from theorem
2
by definition of the Abel Jacobi map and by the base change for mixed hodge modules.
∎

We have the following main result of
[
3
]
:

Theorem 3
.

Let
f:X\to S
a smooth projective morphism with
S,X\in\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(k)
. Let
d,n\in\mathbb{N}
.
Let
\sigma:k\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
an embedding.
For
Z\in\mathcal{Z}^{d}(X,n)^{f,\partial=0}_{fhom}
, the zero locus
V(\nu_{Z})\subset S_{\mathbb{C}}
of

\mu_{Z}:=AJ_{\sigma}(X/S)(Z)\in\Gamma(S_{\mathbb{C}}^{an},J_{\sigma}^{2d-1-n,d}(X/S))

is an algebraic subvariety.

Proof.

See
[
3
]
: if
\bar{S}\in\mathop{\rm PSmVar}\nolimits(k)
is a compactification of
S

with
\bar{S}\backslash S=\cup_{i}D_{i}\subset\bar{S}
a normal crossing divisor,
there exist an analytic subset
\Sigma(\nu_{Z})\subset\bar{S}_{\mathbb{C}}
such that

V(\nu_{Z})=\Sigma(\nu_{Z})\cap S_{\mathbb{C}}
.
By GAGA
\Sigma(\nu_{Z})\subset\bar{S}_{\mathbb{C}}
is algebraic subvariety.
Hence
V(\nu_{Z})\subset S_{\mathbb{C}}
is an algebraic subvariety.
∎

4.2
The etale Abel Jacobi map for higher Chow group and etale normal functions

Let
k
a field of finite type over
\mathbb{Q}
.
Let
\bar{k}
the algebraic closure of
k
and denote by
G=Gal(\bar{k}/k)
its galois group.
Let
p\in\mathbb{N}
a prime integer.

Definition 4
.

Let
X\in\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(k)
irreducible.
Let
\bar{X}\in\mathop{\rm PSmVar}\nolimits(k)
a compactification of
X
with
D:=\bar{X}\backslash X\subset\bar{X}
a normal crossing divisor.
Denote
G=Gal(\bar{k}/k)
the absolute galois group. The cycle class map

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{et,p}_{X}:\mathcal{Z}^{d}(X,n)^{\partial=0}\to H^{2d-n}_{p_{X}(|Z|),et}(X,D,\hat{\mathbb{Z}_{p}})\to H_{et}^{2d-n}(X,D,\hat{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}),

to continuous etale cohomology induces the etale Abel Jacobi map for higher Chow groups

\displaystyle AJ_{et,p}(X):\mathcal{Z}^{d}(X,n)^{\partial=0}_{hom}\to\mathop{\rm Ext}\nolimits^{1}_{G}(\bar{k},H_{et}^{2d-1-n}(X_{\bar{k}},D_{\bar{k}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})),

\displaystyle Z\mapsto AJ_{et,p}(X)(Z):=L^{1}\mathcal{R}^{et,p}_{X}(Z)/L^{2}\mathcal{R}^{et,p}_{X}(Z),

where
L
is the filtration given by the Leray spectral sequence of the map of sites
a_{X}::X^{et}\to\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(k)^{et}
.

Theorem 4
.

Let
X\in\mathop{\rm PSmVar}\nolimits(k)
. Denote
G=Gal(\bar{k}/k)
the absolute galois group.

(i)

For

, we have

\displaystyle AJ_{et,p}(X)(Z)=(0\to H_{et}^{2d-1-n}(X_{\bar{k}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})\xrightarrow{j^{*}}H_{et}^{2d-1}(((X\times\square^{n})\backslash|Z|)_{\bar{k}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{[Z]}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\partial}H_{|Z|,et}^{2d}((X\times\square^{n})_{\bar{k}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{[Z]}=\bar{k}(n-d)\to 0)

with

the open embedding, and

\displaystyle H_{|Z|,et}^{2d}((X\times\square^{n})_{\bar{k}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{[Z]}\subset H_{|Z|,et}^{2d}((X\times\square^{n})_{\bar{k}},\mathbb{Z}_{p}),

\displaystyle H_{et}^{2d-1}(((X\times\square^{n})\backslash|Z|)_{\bar{k}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{[Z]}\subset H_{et}^{2d-1}(((X\times\square^{n})\backslash|Z|)_{\bar{k}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})

are the subobjects given by the pullback by the class of
Z
(see section 2).

(ii)

Z\in\mathcal{Z}^{d}(X,n)^{\partial=0}_{hom}
. Then
AJ_{et,p}(X)(Z)=0
if and only if there exist

such that
\alpha\in H_{et}^{2d-1}(((X\times\square^{n})\backslash|Z|)_{\bar{k}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{G}

and
\partial\alpha\neq 0
.

Proof.

(i):See
[
15
]
.

(ii): Follows from (i).

5
The vanishing of the etale Abel Jacobi map implies the vanishing of the complex Abel Jacobi map

The
p
adic Hodge theory for open varieties implies the following main theorem :

Theorem 6
.

Let
k
a field of finite type over
\mathbb{Q}
.

\bar{k}
the algebraic closure of

and

obviously (i) implies (ii),

It implies the following :

Corollary 2
.

(i)

Let
k
a field of finite type over
\mathbb{Q}
. Denote
\bar{k}
the algebraic closure of
k
.
Let
f:X\to S
a smooth projective morphism with
S,X\in\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(k)
.
Consider an embedding
\sigma:k\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
.
Then we have
\bar{k}\subset\mathbb{C}
the canonical algebraic closure of
k
inside
\mathbb{C}
.
Let
p\in\mathbb{N}
a prime number. Then for
Z\in\mathcal{Z}^{d}(X,n)_{fhom}^{f,\partial=0}
, we have

V_{tors}(\nu^{et,p}_{Z})_{\mathbb{C}}\subset V_{tors}(\nu_{Z})\subset S_{\mathbb{C}}

where

–

V(\nu_{Z})\subset V_{tors}(\nu_{Z})\subset S_{\mathbb{C}}
is the zero locus, resp. torsion locus,
of the complex normal function

\nu_{Z}=:AJ_{\sigma}(X/S)(Z)\in\Gamma(S^{an}_{\mathbb{C}},J_{\sigma}^{2d-1-n,d}(X/S))

associated to
Z
(see proposition-definition
1
),

–

V(\nu^{et,p}_{Z})\subset V_{tors}(\nu^{et,p}_{Z})\subset S
is the zero locus, resp. torsion locus
of the etale normal function

\nu^{et,p}_{Z}\in\Gamma(S,\oplus_{s\in S_{(0)}}i_{s*}\mathop{\rm Ext}\nolimits^{1}_{Gal(\bar{k}/k(s))}(\bar{k},H_{et}^{2d-n-1}(X_{s,\bar{k}},\mathbb{Z}_{p}))(d-n))

associated to
Z
(see definition
5
) and

V(\nu^{et,p}_{Z})_{\mathbb{C}}:=\pi_{k/\mathbb{C}}(S)^{-1}(V(\nu^{et,p}_{Z})),\;V_{tors}(\nu^{et,p}_{Z})_{\mathbb{C}}:=\pi_{k/\mathbb{C}}(S)^{-1}(V_{tors}(\nu^{et,p}_{Z}))

where we recall
\pi_{k/\mathbb{C}}(S):S_{\mathbb{C}}\to S
is the projection.

(ii)

Let
\sigma:k\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
a subfield which is of finite type over
\mathbb{Q}
.
Denote
\bar{k}\subset\mathbb{C}
the algebraic closure of
k
.
Let
f:X\to S
a smooth projective morphism with
S,X\in\mathop{\rm SmVar}\nolimits(k)
.
Then for
Z\in\mathcal{Z}^{d}(X,n)_{fhom}^{f,\partial=0}
,
the zero locus
V(\nu_{Z})\subset S_{\mathbb{C}}
of the complex normal function

\nu_{Z}=:AJ_{\sigma}(X/S)(Z)\in\Gamma(S^{an}_{\mathbb{C}},J_{\sigma}^{2d-1-n,d}(X/S))

associated to
Z
is defined over
\bar{k}
if
V(\nu^{et,p}_{Z})\neq\emptyset
.

Proof.

(i):Follows immediately from theorem
6
since
for
s\in S_{(0)}
,
k(s)
is of finite type over
\mathbb{Q}
and for

denoting
\sigma^{\prime}:k(s)\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
the embedding given by
s^{\prime}
, we have by definition

•

\nu_{Z}(s^{\prime})=:AJ_{\sigma}(X/S)(Z)(s^{\prime}):=AJ_{\sigma^{\prime}}(X_{s})(Z_{s})\in J_{\sigma^{\prime}}^{2d-1-n,d}(X_{s})
.

•

\nu^{et,p}_{Z}(s)=:AJ_{et,p}(X/S)(Z)(s):=AJ_{et,p}(X_{s})(Z_{s})\in\mathop{\rm Ext}\nolimits^{1}_{Gal(\bar{k}/k(s))}(\bar{k},H_{et}^{2d-n-1}(X_{s,\bar{k}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})(d-n))
.

(ii): Since

contain a
\bar{k}
point,

V(\nu_{Z})\subset S_{\mathbb{C}}
contain a
\bar{k}
point by (i). Hence by
the work of
[
18
]
or
[
